## intake-2407#record — Entire CLI (entireio/cli): git-native, commit-linked capture of AI agent sessions as per-checkpoint refs

- **URL**: https://github.com/entireio/cli
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, knowledge_management, context_management, tool_implementation)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-2387#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Capture and linking (README Key Concepts, docs/architecture): agent hooks (Claude Code .claude/settings.json, Codex .codex/hooks.json, OpenCode .opencode/plugins/entire.ts TypeScript plugin, Cursor, Pi, others) feed session state; a checkpoint is created only when a git commit happens and the commit message carries an Entire-Checkpoint: <26-char ULID> trailer; the checkpoint is its own git ref refs/entire/checkpoints/<shard>/<id> whose tree holds metadata.json, per-session transcript files and subagent task records.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh. The SWE-chat paper (intake-2397#record) describes a "dedicated branch"; current CLI stores per-checkpoint refs (git-branch is the legacy backend) - narrowing, not conflict.
   - anchor `README.md Checkpoints` @ github.com/entireio/cli @eb51bb1d7e81b62bd956d9a8409aa47f1e23d887 (2026-10-22, MIT): "Checkpoints are created when you or the agent make a git commit, and the commit carries an `Entire-Checkpoint: <id>` trailer linking the two."
   - anchor `README.md Checkpoint Storage` @ github.com/entireio/cli @eb51bb1d7e81b62bd956d9a8409aa47f1e23d887 (2026-10-22, MIT): "The ref points at a commit whose tree *is* that checkpoint — `metadata.json`, the per-session transcript files, and any subagent task records."
   - anchor `README.md Agent Hook Configuration` @ github.com/entireio/cli @eb51bb1d7e81b62bd956d9a8409aa47f1e23d887 (2026-10-22, MIT): "| OpenCode | `.opencode/plugins/entire.ts` | TypeScript plugin |"
1. Retention model: there is no TTL, expiry or retention knob for transcripts (grep for retention/ttl/expire over README.md and docs/ finds only a catalog-index cache, auth-token expiry, an Antigravity 14-day snapshot cache and git gc pruneExpire for deleted shadow branches). Committed checkpoints live as long as the git object store does and are pushed with the code by default (strategy_options.push_sessions default true), or to a separate private repo via strategy_options.checkpoint_remote.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh absence claim; trees searched: README.md, docs/*.md, docs/architecture/*.md @eb51bb1d.
   - anchor `README.md Configuration Options` @ github.com/entireio/cli @eb51bb1d7e81b62bd956d9a8409aa47f1e23d887 (2026-10-22, MIT): "| `strategy_options.push_sessions` | `true`, `false` | Auto-push checkpoint data on git push (default `true`) |"
   - anchor `README.md Security & Privacy` @ github.com/entireio/cli @eb51bb1d7e81b62bd956d9a8409aa47f1e23d887 (2026-10-22, MIT): "**Your session transcripts are stored in your git repository**, in the [per-checkpoint refs](#checkpoint-storage) described above."
2. Coverage gap for sessions that never commit: between commits nothing is written to git; pending work sits in .git/entire-sessions/<id>.json and a sanitized local .entire/metadata/<session>/full.jsonl. Persistent checkpoints without a commit exist only via eager condensation at session end, doctor/sweeper salvage and hidden snapshots for sessions that change no files, so research/planning/review sessions depend on those paths. The paper entry intake-2397#record independently notes that fully abandoned sessions are never captured.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh.
   - anchor `docs/security-and-privacy.md L20` @ github.com/entireio/cli @eb51bb1d7e81b62bd956d9a8409aa47f1e23d887 (2026-10-22, MIT): "Between commits, Entire writes nothing to git."
   - anchor `docs/architecture/sessions-and-checkpoints.md` @ github.com/entireio/cli @eb51bb1d7e81b62bd956d9a8409aa47f1e23d887 (2026-10-22, MIT): "Most persistent checkpoints are written when a commit condenses a session, but some have no commit: eager condensation at session end, `entire doctor` and the session sweeper, and snapshots from the hidden `entire checkpoint create`."
3. Backfill is narrow: the experimental, hidden entire import <agent> reads pre-existing transcripts as read-only checkpoints but with a fixed LookbackDays = 30 (by transcript file mtime, no flag), per repository (Codex sessions matched by session_meta cwd), and the registered importers are claude-code, cursor, pi, factory, codex, copilot only: no OpenCode importer and no ~/.claude/history.jsonl, so it cannot recover anything that has already rolled past the agent's own cleanup.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh. Corrects the assumption that import could rescue our rolled-off history: the lookback equals the agent cleanup horizon.
   - anchor `cmd/entire/cli/agentimport/agentimport.go L30-L31` @ github.com/entireio/cli @eb51bb1d7e81b62bd956d9a8409aa47f1e23d887 (2026-10-22, MIT): "// LookbackDays bounds how far back import reaches. Fixed this pass (no flag). const LookbackDays = 30"
   - anchor `cmd/entire/cli/agentimport/agentimport.go L92-L99` @ github.com/entireio/cli @eb51bb1d7e81b62bd956d9a8409aa47f1e23d887 (2026-10-22, MIT): "var importers = []Importer{ claudeImporter{}, cursorImporter{}, piImporter{}, factoryImporter{}, codexImporter{}, copilotImporter{},"
   - anchor `cmd/entire/cli/import_cmd.go` @ github.com/entireio/cli @eb51bb1d7e81b62bd956d9a8409aa47f1e23d887 (2026-10-22, MIT): "Import pre-existing %s transcripts for this repo (the past month) as read-only checkpoints."
4. Redaction is irreversible and text-only: secrets are replaced by REDACTED before any git object is written (entropy, Betterleaks patterns, credentialed URIs, DB DSNs and more), but the local full.jsonl copy is unredacted (mode 0600), pasted images are stored unredacted for Claude Code, and the docs state the whole scheme is best-effort; anyone with repo access, or the internet if the repo is public, can read committed transcripts.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh.
   - anchor `docs/security-and-privacy.md Local copies` @ github.com/entireio/cli @eb51bb1d7e81b62bd956d9a8409aa47f1e23d887 (2026-10-22, MIT): "`.entire/metadata/<session>/full.jsonl` is Entire's own local working copy of the transcript, written mode `0600`. It is *sanitized* (agent state that cannot be replayed out of a checkpoint is stripped) but **not redacted**"
   - anchor `docs/security-and-privacy.md images` @ github.com/entireio/cli @eb51bb1d7e81b62bd956d9a8409aa47f1e23d887 (2026-10-22, MIT): "| Claude Code | **Stored unredacted**, inline in the transcript as base64."
5. Optional LLM summaries at commit time (strategy_options.summarize, default provider Claude Code, selectable codex/opencode/pi/cursor/copilot-cli) write intent, outcome, learnings {repo, code, workflow} and friction points per checkpoint; nothing in the CLI records whether a learning was later retrieved, followed or reduced friction (grep outcome/efficacy/measure over runner_*.go and the summary code: no such field), and the runner-tuning code reads CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md only as capped input context (6000 chars), not as an object it evaluates.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh absence claim; trees searched: cmd/entire/cli/runner_*.go, explain*.go, api/checkpoint/metadata.go.
   - anchor `README.md Auto-Summarization` @ github.com/entireio/cli @eb51bb1d7e81b62bd956d9a8409aa47f1e23d887 (2026-10-22, MIT): "Summaries capture intent, outcome, learnings, friction points, and open items from the session."
   - anchor `api/checkpoint/metadata.go L654-L659` @ github.com/entireio/cli @eb51bb1d7e81b62bd956d9a8409aa47f1e23d887 (2026-10-22, MIT): "type LearningsSummary struct { Repo []string `json:"repo"` // Codebase-specific patterns/conventions Code []CodeLearning `json:"code"` // File/module specific findings Workflow []string `json:"workflow"` // General dev practices }"
   - anchor `cmd/entire/cli/runner_gather.go L20` @ github.com/entireio/cli @eb51bb1d7e81b62bd956d9a8409aa47f1e23d887 (2026-10-22, MIT): "tuneDocCap = 6000 // max chars embedded per doc (CLAUDE.md etc.)"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): Entire is a capture-and-link layer, not a retention-past-cleanup rescue: forward capture is durable (git objects, no TTL) but backfill is capped at 30 days, per repo, and excludes OpenCode and history.jsonl, and sessions that never commit are only partly covered. It carries no lesson-efficacy measurement; its learnings/friction summaries are the same kind of unmeasured distilled lesson as Beacon's.

## intake-2408#record — Agent Memory: Characterization and System Implications of Stateful Long-Horizon Workloads

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06448
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance low; categories: memory_augmented, knowledge_management, benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2387#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Scope: a systems characterization (not a quality study) of ten agent-memory systems in four paradigms (long-context, flat RAG, structure-augmented RAG, agentic/LLM-mediated) on MemoryAgentBench (LongMemEval_S_* centre: 5 samples of ~360K tokens, 300 queries) and MemoryArena, with a phase-aware profiler that attributes tokens, calls, latency and GPU energy to construction, retrieval and generation; every benchmark is recall-accuracy over conversational or long-context history, none measures whether stored memory changes later agent behaviour, and none is a coding-agent workload.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh; absence claim (no behaviour-change evaluation) from a search of the v2 text for lesson/behavio/coding/instruction terms.
   - anchor `Sec. 1` @ arXiv:2606.06448v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-09-22, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 013c58c8c46e): "Existing benchmarks evaluate these systems purely on downstream accuracy [14, 28, 6, 27], leaving their system-level behavior uncharacterized."
   - anchor `Sec. 3.1` @ arXiv:2606.06448v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-09-22, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 013c58c8c46e): "This workload contains five samples, each with approximately 360 K tokens of history and 60 queries (300 total), cleanly separating construction over a long interaction history from repeated query-time retrieval and generation."
1. Headline: construction, not query-time serving, dominates lifecycle cost for LLM-mediated memories; end-to-end energy spans 26.5x from BM25 (582 kJ) to Letta (15,429 kJ) on LongMemEval with Qwen3-32B, and per-query latency differs by two orders of magnitude on identical hardware.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh; arithmetic checked: 15,429 / 582 = 26.5.
   - anchor `Sec. 4.2` @ arXiv:2606.06448v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-09-22, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 013c58c8c46e): "Table 3 shows that end-to-end energy span is over 26.5×, from BM25 at 582 kJ to Letta at 15,429 kJ."
   - anchor `Sec. 5` @ arXiv:2606.06448v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-09-22, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 013c58c8c46e): "we find that the dominant cost in agent memory is not query-time serving but construction"
2. Lexical baseline wins on accuracy per joule: on the same run BM25 scores 47.0% accuracy at 582 kJ versus GraphRAG 46.0, HippoRAG v2 44.3, A-Mem 42.7, embedRAG 39.8, SimpleMem 36.0, Mem0 32.0, Letta 27.7, MIRIX 20.0; none of the LLM-mediated constructions beats the no-LLM index on accuracy in this setting, and the accuracy differences among the top rows are small relative to the sample size (5 samples, 300 queries; no confidence intervals in the text read).
   - *unaffected*: Fresh; numbers copied from Table 3 (columns Acc., Wall, Calls, Total kJ, J/correct); the Letta 15,429 kJ and BM25 582 kJ cells match the Sec. 4.2 prose.
   - anchor `Sec. 4.2 Table 3 (BM25 row)` @ arXiv:2606.06448v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-09-22, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 013c58c8c46e): "BM25 47.0 16.3m 300 582 4,128"
   - anchor `Sec. 4.2 Table 3 (Mem0 row)` @ arXiv:2606.06448v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-09-22, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 013c58c8c46e): "Mem0 32.0 4.02h 4,538 4,878 50,813"
3. Capability floor: systems with strict output contracts fail structurally when the construction model is too small (MIRIX collapses at Qwen3-1.7B because malformed JSON/tool calls corrupt the store), while systems without hard contracts degrade smoothly; the paper's recommendation is to validate the minimum viable construction LLM before deployment.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh.
   - anchor `Sec. 4.4 Insight 4` @ arXiv:2606.06448v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-09-22, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 013c58c8c46e): "MIRIX fails entirely at Qwen3-1.7B, because its pipeline relies on multiple sub-agent tool calls that require well-formed JSON schemas and legal tool-call syntax."
4. Maintenance is the weak stage: the authors state that in many current systems memory accumulates indefinitely with no freshness or pruning policy, and per-user footprint growth diverges across systems (Sec. 4.7); inter-session construction creates a freshness-latency trade-off when writes are slower than session arrival (Sec. 4.6).
   - *unaffected*: Fresh.
   - anchor `Sec. 2.1 Maintenance` @ arXiv:2606.06448v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-09-22, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 013c58c8c46e): "In many current systems, maintenance is weak or absent: memory accumulates indefinitely with no freshness or pruning policy"
5. Artifact: phase-aware profiling harness plus the ten systems and scripts are released at github.com/Tambe-Lab/agent-memory-profiling (archived at Zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.21846239); repo licence and contents were not fetched (GitHub API rate-limited), so reuse is unverified.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh; repo not fetched.
   - anchor `Artifact appendix` @ arXiv:2606.06448v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-09-22, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 013c58c8c46e): "The artifact is available as a GitHub repository: https://github.com/Tambe-Lab/agent-memory-profiling."

### Dive corrections

## intake-2409#record — Evaluating Memory Condensation Strategies for Coding Agents in Data-Driven Scientific Discovery

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18854
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance low; categories: context_management, agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-2387#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Design: eight OpenHands condensers (NoOp, RecentEvents, AmortizedForgetting, ObservationMasking, ConversationWindow, LLMSummarizing, StructuredSummary, LLMAttention) compared on DiscoveryBench, 10 test tasks in each of 6 domains = 60 tasks x 8 = 480 runs, one backbone (GPT-4o via Azure, temperature 0), LLM condensers use GPT-5-mini, quality judged by GPT-4o against gold hypotheses.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh; low-priority paper, read in full text.
   - anchor `Sec. 3.2` @ arXiv:2605.18854v1 (HTML, 2026-05-13, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 e9ca106f05d3): "Our evaluation uses stratified sampling: 10 tasks per domain (test split only) across six domains (60 total) yielding 480 evaluations in total (60×8 condensers)."
1. Scale caveat that bounds every result: the tasks are SHORT (5-22 events, NoOp mean 9.0 events, about 75k tokens) and condensation triggers at max_size=8, so the study is a regime of barely-over-threshold histories, not hundreds-of-turns coding sessions; the authors list this as limitation 2.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh; this is the main narrowing on how far results port to long coding sessions.
   - anchor `Sec. 3.4` @ arXiv:2605.18854v1 (HTML, 2026-05-13, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 e9ca106f05d3): "which averages 9 events per task (range: 5–22) and roughly 75k tokens. Based on these statistics, we set max_size=8"
   - anchor `Sec. 6` @ arXiv:2605.18854v1 (HTML, 2026-05-13, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 e9ca106f05d3): "Second, DiscoveryBench tasks range from 5–22 events; real-world scientific workflows involving hundreds of iterations may amplify or shift the patterns we observe"
2. Cost: only ObservationMasking (8.6% fewer tokens than NoOp, quality 0.572) and ConversationWindow (1.2%) save tokens overall; every LLM-based condenser increases total tokens (LLMSummarizing +23.6%, StructuredSummary +59.0%, LLMAttention +93.9%) and 41-70% of tasks need more events, which the authors attribute to agents losing context and retrying.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh; the paper text says "+8.6%" for savings in one place and Table 3 shows -23.6% etc. as savings sign, both consistent with the signs used here.
   - anchor `Sec. 4 Table 3` @ arXiv:2605.18854v1 (HTML, 2026-05-13, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 e9ca106f05d3): "ObservationMasking 68,718 8.6%"
   - anchor `Sec. 4 Table 3` @ arXiv:2605.18854v1 (HTML, 2026-05-13, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 e9ca106f05d3): "LLMAttention 145,738 −93.9%"
3. Quality: no condenser changes hypothesis quality significantly (range 0.559-0.637 vs NoOp 0.591, paired Wilcoxon p>0.05 Bonferroni-corrected, overlapping CIs); with n=60 paired tasks this is absence of evidence, not equivalence (no power or minimum-detectable-effect analysis is reported).
   - *unaffected*: Fresh; "absence of evidence" wording is ours - the paper concludes "cost, not quality, differentiates strategies".
   - anchor `Sec. 4.1` @ arXiv:2605.18854v1 (HTML, 2026-05-13, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 e9ca106f05d3): "No condenser produces a statistically significant change in hypothesis quality."
4. Task length and domain matter: only long tasks (>10 events) yield positive savings for heuristic condensers (17-34%; ObservationMasking +33.5%), LLM condensers stay net-negative even there except LLMSummarizing at about break-even (+0.4%), and ObservationMasking saves 52% in verbose sociology tasks but nothing in compact meta-science.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh.
   - anchor `Sec. 5` @ arXiv:2605.18854v1 (HTML, 2026-05-13, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 e9ca106f05d3): "Only long tasks (>10 events) yield positive savings for heuristic condensers (17–34%), while short tasks see increased costs across all strategies."
5. Relation to prior work: this replicates, on a different harness (OpenHands CodeAct vs SWE-agent) and domain (scientific discovery vs SWE-bench), the Complexity Trap finding already in the index as intake-274#record that simple observation masking matches or beats LLM summarization; it is a corroboration, not new mechanism.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh; the paper itself does not cite intake-274#record (arXiv 2508.21433) in the passages read - the link is ours.
   - anchor `Sec. 1` @ arXiv:2605.18854v1 (HTML, 2026-05-13, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 e9ca106f05d3): "none of these works compare multiple condensation strategies while controlling for task type and model."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): useful only as a second, independent-harness data point that heuristic observation masking is the cheap, safe condenser and LLM condensers cost more than they save at short horizons; it says nothing about the 100+-event regime our harnesses actually run in, and it does not touch lesson memory.

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2410#record — Experiential Reflective Learning for Self-Improving LLM Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.24639
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: memory_augmented, agent_architecture, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2387#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Method: after each single-attempt task the agent reflects on trajectory plus a binary outcome into a structured heuristic (analysis + guideline with explicit trigger conditions and recommended actions); at test time an LLM scores the pool and the top-k (k=20) are injected into the system prompt, with no change to the ReAct loop.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh.
   - anchor `Sec. 2 Heuristic generation` @ arXiv:2603.24639v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-03-31, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 d9df614c3c36): "a learned guideline with explicit trigger conditions and recommended actions"
   - anchor `Sec. 2 Retrieval` @ arXiv:2603.24639v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-03-31, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 d9df614c3c36): "The top-k heuristics are then injected into the agent’s system prompt, ensuring that the agent receives task-specific advice rather than being overwhelmed with the full heuristic pool."
1. This IS a lesson-efficacy A/B on outcomes, which narrows the wave-1 statement that no source measures whether hand-kept lessons change behaviour: on Gaia2 Search+Execution, heuristics accumulated on 8 universes are tested on 2 held-out universes (48 execution + 28 search tasks, 3 runs, GPT-5-mini) and raise success from 48.3% to 56.1% (7.8 points). The unit is task success of a pool, not violation recurrence of an individual lesson, and no confidence interval or significance test is reported.
   - *unaffected*: NARROWS the wave-1 premise "no source measures lesson efficacy": ERL is a pool-level outcome A/B with a disjoint held-out split; it still does not measure per-lesson violation recurrence or an operator-correction rate. The abstract/intro phrase "+7.8%" means percentage points (56.1 - 48.3), not relative gain.
   - anchor `Sec. 3 Experimental setup` @ arXiv:2603.24639v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-03-31, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 d9df614c3c36): "we accumulate heuristics on 8 universes (112 execution / 132 search tasks) and evaluate on 2 held-out test universes (48 / 28 tasks)."
   - anchor `Sec. 3 Figure 2(a)` @ arXiv:2603.24639v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-03-31, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 d9df614c3c36): "Baseline 43.1 53.6 48.3"
   - anchor `Sec. 3 Figure 2(a)` @ arXiv:2603.24639v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-03-31, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 d9df614c3c36): "ERL (Ours) 51.4 60.7 56.1"
2. Selection beats dumping and bloat hurts: random k heuristics peak at about 40-60 and then degrade; LLM-selected k=20 (56.1) beats embedding retrieval (53.3) and the best random configuration (53.8); raw-trajectory few-shot is below baseline (46.4); but ablation gaps of 2-3 points sit inside the noise of a 76-task, 3-run test set.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh; the 2-3 point ablation gaps vs ~3+ point binomial standard error (our arithmetic: sqrt(0.5*0.5/228) = 3.3 points, larger with task clustering) are noise-level; the baseline-vs-ERL 7.8 points is about 2 SE.
   - anchor `Sec. 3` @ arXiv:2603.24639v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-03-31, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 d9df614c3c36): "Performance exhibits a non-monotonic pattern, peaking around 40–60 heuristics before degrading with excessive inclusion."
   - anchor `Sec. 3 Figure 2(b)` @ arXiv:2603.24639v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-03-31, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 d9df614c3c36): "embedding retrieval 50.7 56.0 53.3"
3. Failure-derived versus success-derived lessons split by task type: failures-only reach 58.9 overall (Search 67.9, Execution 50.0) while successes-only reach 49.9 (Execution 52.1, Search 47.6); the authors advise using both when the task mix is unknown.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh.
   - anchor `Sec. 3 Figure 2(b)` @ arXiv:2603.24639v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-03-31, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 d9df614c3c36): "only failures 50.0 67.9 58.9"
   - anchor `Sec. 3 Figure 2(b)` @ arXiv:2603.24639v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-03-31, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 d9df614c3c36): "only successes 52.1 47.6 49.9"
4. Outcome signal quality: without environment rewards the agent mislabels its own outcome 30% of the time (70% correct) and ERL falls to 51.2 (still above the 48.3 baseline); on tau2-bench ERL is +1.3 points overall (38.0 vs 36.7) with Telecom worse (28.3 vs 30.0, pass^3 to zero), and an iterative variant generalizes worse to held-out universes (50.7 vs 56.1).
   - *unaffected*: Fresh.
   - anchor `App. C.2` @ arXiv:2603.24639v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-03-31, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 d9df614c3c36): "In this setting, the agent correctly identifies success or failure only 70% of the time, and the success rate of ERL drops to 51.2% (-4.8%)."
   - anchor `App. E Table 5` @ arXiv:2603.24639v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-03-31, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 d9df614c3c36): "Iterative ERL achieves higher source task performance but lower test generalization."
5. Cost: injecting about 20k tokens of heuristics on top of a ~12k-token system prompt at every turn nearly doubles rollout input tokens (+85%) and raises total API cost 40% (rollout +15% after prompt caching, plus generation and retrieval calls); turns per scenario are unchanged (16.6 vs 17.6).
   - *unaffected*: Fresh.
   - anchor `App. D` @ arXiv:2603.24639v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-03-31, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 d9df614c3c36): "Input tokens for scenario rollout nearly double (+85%), primarily because retrieved heuristics (∼20k tokens) are appended to the base system prompt (∼12k tokens) at every turn"
   - anchor `App. D` @ arXiv:2603.24639v2 (HTML, v2 of 2026-03-31, retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 d9df614c3c36): "Accounting for heuristic generation and retrieval, ERL incurs a 40% overall increase in API costs."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): ERL is the one source in this group that runs a held-out A/B of injected lessons on task outcomes, so it supplies a design template (disjoint-universe split, pass^3 reliability, random-k and reward-free controls, token-cost line) for our lesson-efficacy study; but its effect sizes are single-digit points on 76 tasks without uncertainty, and nothing in it observes whether a specific lesson stopped a specific mistake.

- created `handoffs/active/dev-harness-trace-mining.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/unified-trace-memory-service.md`

## intake-2411#record — vllm-continuum: vLLM with Continuum scheduling (KV pin with TTL, program-level FCFS)

- **URL**: https://github.com/Hanchenli/vllm-continuum
- **Verification**: dive-overturned
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: inference_serving, kv_cache, agent_architecture, tool_implementation)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2398#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The repository README states that the released code is Continuum-style scheduling WITHOUT the estimation described in the paper, and says multi-node use needs sticky-session routing (README.md L3).
   - *overturned*: Stage-1 premise (code implements per-tool-CDF argmax TTL) is overturned by the README and the code.
   - anchor `README.md:L3 @ 316a587` @ Hanchenli/vllm-continuum @ 316a58794a6ff86b216e579b74fd56ed0c5a911f (main, 2026-03-19), cloned 2026-10-22: "This repository contains a modified version of vLLM with Continuum-style scheduling support (without the estimation in the paper) for improved inference performance. For multi-node support, you should use sticky-session routing."
1. The shipped TTL logic is a fixed 2.0 s pin gate: a finished request that parsed to a tool call is pinned for exactly FIXED_THRESHOLD_CONTINUUM = 2.0 s when that tool name's mean observed duration is <= 2.0 s, and not pinned otherwise; a tool name with no history counts as 0.0 s and IS pinned (estimate_with_func.py L10, L154-159). There is no empirical CDF, no argmax over candidate TTLs, no queueing-delay term, no memoryfulness factor, no reload-cost profile and no K=100 cold-start ladder anywhere in the tree (searched vllm/, continuum_exp/, test_scripts/, mini-swe-agent/src).
   - *narrowed*: TTL is binary-gated fixed 2.0 s.
   - anchor `vllm/v1/core/estimate_with_func.py:L154-L159 @ 316a587` @ Hanchenli/vllm-continuum @ 316a58794a6ff86b216e579b74fd56ed0c5a911f (main, 2026-03-19), cloned 2026-10-22: "this_func_call_exec_time = self.get_func_call_exec_time(request.this_func_call) or 0.0 if this_func_call_exec_time > FIXED_THRESHOLD_CONTINUUM: return 0 return FIXED_THRESHOLD_CONTINUUM"
2. The per-tool duration statistic is a running arithmetic mean of (next-request arrival - previous-request departure) keyed by tool name, labelled TODO "currently just an average" by the author (estimate_with_func.py L135-146); the interval includes client, network and harness time, not only tool execution.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh claim.
   - anchor `vllm/v1/core/estimate_with_func.py:L135-L146 @ 316a587` @ Hanchenli/vllm-continuum @ 316a58794a6ff86b216e579b74fd56ed0c5a911f (main, 2026-03-19), cloned 2026-10-22: "#TODO Hanchen This is currently just an average def update_func_call_exec_time(self, job_id: str) -> None: #this is called when the func call is back again in scheduler.py, update the exec time with last_func_call last_departure_time = self.job_to_history[job_id][-1]["departure_time"] func = self..."
3. Pin mechanics are an engine-side list pinned_requests of (request, expiry): _free_blocks skips kv_cache_manager.free for a pinned request; every schedule step unpins entries whose expiry passed AND whose job_id has no request in the waiting queue; the last step of a job and a pool-exhaustion deadlock unpin early (scheduler.py L248-256, L1357-1364).
   - *unaffected*: Fresh claim.
   - anchor `vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py:L1357-L1364 and L246-L256 @ 316a587` @ Hanchenli/vllm-continuum @ 316a58794a6ff86b216e579b74fd56ed0c5a911f (main, 2026-03-19), cloned 2026-10-22: "############# if self.policy == SchedulingPolicy.CONTINUUM and not request.is_last_step: length_of_pin = self.tool_call_estimator.set_up_pin(request) #print(f"Setting up pin for request {request.request_id} with length {length_of_pin}") #Floating point error if length_of_pin > 0.01: self.pin_requ..."
4. Program-level FCFS: the waiting queue serves pinned jobs first (earliest job first-arrival), otherwise the request whose job_id first arrived earliest (request_queue.py L233-258). Preemption under CONTINUUM avoids last-step jobs and, when <= 1 request is running, unpins the pinned request with the LATEST EXPIRY (scheduler.py L196-207), not the latest program arrival as the paper text says.
   - *narrowed*: Eviction order differs from the paper text (latest expiry vs latest program arrival).
   - anchor `vllm/v1/core/sched/request_queue.py:L233-L258 @ 316a587` @ Hanchenli/vllm-continuum @ 316a58794a6ff86b216e579b74fd56ed0c5a911f (main, 2026-03-19), cloned 2026-10-22: "def pop_request(self, pinned_requests: list[Tuple[Request, float]], kv_cache_manager: KVCacheManager, connector: KVConnectorBase_V1) -> Request: """Pop a request from the queue according to continuum policy.""" request = self.peek_request(pinned_requests, kv_cache_manager, connector) self.remove_..."

5. Program identity and the last-step hint are CLIENT-SUPPLIED request fields (job_id, is_last_step added to the OpenAI protocol; mini-swe-agent computes is_last_step from its step_limit). The server cannot infer program boundaries on its own in the released code.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh claim.
   - anchor `vllm/entrypoints/openai/protocol.py:L293-L296 and mini-swe-agent/src/minisweagent/models/vllm_model.py:L124-L127 @ 316a587` @ Hanchenli/vllm-continuum @ 316a58794a6ff86b216e579b74fd56ed0c5a911f (main, 2026-03-19), cloned 2026-10-22: "# Custom fields for job/function-call metadata job_id: Optional[str] = None last_func_call: Optional[str] = None is_last_step: Optional[bool] = None # Calculate is_last_step based on step_limit is_last_step = False if self.config.step_limit > 0: is_last_step = (self.n_calls + 1) >= self.config.st..."
6. The tool-name parser is specific to mini-swe-agent output (first word of a single ```bash fenced block); other agents need their own parser (estimate_with_func.py L73-92).
   - *unaffected*: Fresh claim.
   - anchor `vllm/v1/core/estimate_with_func.py:L73-L92 @ 316a587` @ Hanchenli/vllm-continuum @ 316a58794a6ff86b216e579b74fd56ed0c5a911f (main, 2026-03-19), cloned 2026-10-22: "def parse(self, text: str) -> Optional[str]: """Parse LLM output and extract the function call name. 7. Portability read: the estimator plus TTL arithmetic is ~60 lines of engine-independent Python and can live in an orchestrator layer; the pin enforcement is welded to vLLM's block manager and cannot be lifted, so on llama-server it becomes either a slot-residency policy (--no-cache-idle-slots plus orchestrator-side victim steering) or an experimental-branch server change.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): read the repo at 316a587. The estimation the paper centres on (empirical-CDF argmax TTL) is absent from the released code by the author's own README; only a fixed 2 s pin gate, mean-based tool statistic and program-FCFS are present. ## intake-2412#record — Stateful Large Language Model Serving with Pensieve

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.05516
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance low; categories: inference_serving, kv_cache)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-2398#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Pensieve keeps finished conversations' KV in a GPU tier plus a CPU tier instead of freeing it at request end, and claims 1.14-3.0x the throughput of vLLM and TensorRT-LLM on ShareGPT/UltraChat with OPT-13B and Llama-2-13B on one A100-80GB (Abstract; Sec 6.2).
   - *unaffected*: Fresh claim.
   - anchor `Abstract (derived text L94-L102)` @ arXiv:2312.05516v3 (07 Oct 2024; EuroSys 2025 per its own ACM header), HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 72eb0bd02b4c: "In this paper, we design $Pensieve$ , a system optimized for multi-turn conversation LLM serving. $Pensieve$ maintains the conversation state across requests by caching previously processed history to avoid duplicate processing. $Pensieve$ ’s multi-tier caching strategy can utilize both GPU and C..."
1. Eviction uses a per-32-token-chunk retention value V = Cost(s,l)/T (recompute cost over time since the conversation was last active) and evicts lowest V first, which both prefers idle conversations and drops LEADING tokens first because attention recompute cost grows with context position (Sec 4.3.1).
   - *unaffected*: Fresh claim.
   - anchor `Sec 4.3.1 (derived text L250-L253)` @ arXiv:2312.05516v3 (07 Oct 2024; EuroSys 2025 per its own ACM header), HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 72eb0bd02b4c: "In order to combine both the LRU preference and the evicting from the front preference, we calculate a score for each chunk to capture its retention value. Specifically, the retention value of a chunk is $V=\frac{Cost(s,l)}{T}$ , where $Cost(s,l)$ represents the cost of recomputing a chunk of siz..."
2. Dropping leading tokens forces a two-range prefill (recompute the dropped head plus the new tail, attending over swapped-in middle), which needs a new multi-token attention kernel over non-contiguous KV (Sec 4.3.4, 4.4).
   - *unaffected*: Fresh claim.
   - anchor `Sec 4.3.4 (derived text L284-L286)` @ arXiv:2312.05516v3 (07 Oct 2024; EuroSys 2025 per its own ACM header), HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 72eb0bd02b4c: "If a request has some of its KV-tokens dropped due to CPU memory pressure, we resort to recomputation to handle such dropped tokens. Figure 5 shows the whereabouts of a typical new request’s KV-tokens. As we always evict cached tokens from the leading end of a conversation, the GPU cache generally"
3. Ahead-of-time swapping copies selected KV to CPU when GPU free slots drop below a threshold (e.g. 25%) so eviction overlaps compute, and swap-in is pipelined layer by layer (Sec 4.3.2-4.3.3).
   - *unaffected*: Fresh claim.
   - anchor `Sec 4.3.2 (derived text L267-L270)` @ arXiv:2312.05516v3 (07 Oct 2024; EuroSys 2025 per its own ACM header), HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 72eb0bd02b4c: "Instead of waiting until the GPU cache has run out, the scheduler asks the worker to copy (aka swap out) selected KV-tokens to the CPU if less than a threshold (e.g. 25%) of the GPU cache slots are available. The corresponding GPU memory is reclaimed in a lazy manner and is not immediately releas..."
4. The retention-value policy beats plain LRU only at high load: both match until ~3 req/s, after which it recomputes up to 14.6% fewer KV-tokens and gets up to 4.4 points higher CPU hit rate (Sec 6.6).
   - *unaffected*: Fresh claim.
   - anchor `Sec 6.2 (derived text L498-L503)` @ arXiv:2312.05516v3 (07 Oct 2024; EuroSys 2025 per its own ACM header), HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 72eb0bd02b4c: "Figure 10 shows the normalized latency vs throughput for OPT-13B and Llama 2-13B served with a single A100-80GB GPU. The normalized latency is calculated as the mean of each request’s end-to-end latency divided by its output length, as done in (Yu et al., 2022; Kwon et al., 2023). On the ShareGPT..."
5. No mention of recurrent/SSM/hybrid state anywhere in the text (searched the full HTML text for recurrent, state space, hybrid, mamba): the design assumes every cached token is an independently droppable attention KV entry.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh claim.
   - anchor `Sec 6.6 (derived text L577-L581)` @ arXiv:2312.05516v3 (07 Oct 2024; EuroSys 2025 per its own ACM header), HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 72eb0bd02b4c: "Figure 14 shows that both policies exhibit similar performance until the workload approaches 3 requests per second, beyond which Pensieve’s eviction policy outperforms LRU. After analyzing the execution traces, we find that both policies have less than 80% cache hit rate, however,"

### Dive corrections

0. E2 (exploitation + exploration) global scheduler: match the request in a global radix tree; if the matched prefix is longer than the unmatched remainder, send it to the GPU holding the longest match (lightest load on ties); otherwise place it by minimum cost L_i + M_i + P_i, all expressed as estimated GPU time from token counts via offline regression (Sec 3.2).
   - *unaffected*: Fresh claim.

It first matches the request’s prompt in the global prefix trees. When the amount of recomputation saved (number of tokens in the matched prefix) is larger than the amount of new ..."
1. The eviction-cost term M_i = sum over would-be-evicted nodes of (prefill time of the node x share of window-H requests that hit it), so popular prefixes are expensive to displace; load L_i is summed over a 3-minute history window H (Sec 3.2).

After placement it rebalances (shift future requests of the hottest GPU when its load exceeds Th_bal x the lightest) and autoscales a prefix by replicating it when average queueing time doubles over H; a prefill/decode balancing rule steers uncached (prefill-like) requests to decode-heavy GPUs (Sec 3.2).

## intake-2414#record — LLGuidance: Making Structured Outputs Go Brrr

- **URL**: https://guidance-ai.github.io/llguidance/llg-go-brrr
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: inference_serving, tool_implementation, local_inference, hardware_optimization)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-2388#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Vendor-reported: LLGuidance computes a token mask in ~50 us on average on MaskBench (other libraries 10-1000x slower, partly outlier-driven) with ~2 ms start-up; the speed is attributed to four factors each worth 10-100x: lexer/parser split, token trie, low-level Rust layout, and the novel "slicer" (page intro).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `Introduction ¶ "In MaskBench" @ page` @ retrieved 2026-10-22; page dated 2025-06-11; html sha256-12 7512a5e5463c: "it computes a token mask in about 50μs on average, with other libraries being 10-1000x slower (though in some cases this is due to difficult outliers)."
1. Cost anatomy: the Earley parser is touched by only 0.1-1% of token checks; trie traversal runs ~13 cycles/node (5 reads + 2 writes) on an AMD EPYC 7V13 at 4.2 IPC with <0.1% branch mispredicts; sparse masks are cheap and DENSE masks (inside unconstrained JSON strings/comments) dominate cost, which the slicer short-circuits with precomputed per-slice masks.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `"Counting cycles" @ page` @ retrieved 2026-10-22; page dated 2025-06-11; html sha256-12 7512a5e5463c: "On an AMD EPYC 7V13 (a 128-thread server-class CPU commonly used with A100 GPUs) a single node is processed in around 13 cycles (at 4.2 instructions per cycle);"
2. Over ~2M MaskBench masks: 44.6% are sparse (<=2% of vocab) at 28 us each; 53.8% are dense and sliced at 61 us; the 0.5% dense-unsliced tail averages 1577 us and carries 13% of total time; 1.1% intermediate masks average 576 us (11% of time) (section "Mask density statistics").
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `"Mask density statistics" table @ page` @ retrieved 2026-10-22; page dated 2025-06-11; html sha256-12 7512a5e5463c: "85%+ & sliced 53.8% 55.0% 61"
3. The "negligible overhead" argument assumes the mask is computed on CPU WHILE the GPU runs the forward pass (budget 0.1-10 ms depending on batch/cores); it states the overhead is zero only if compute_mask() finishes before the forward pass. It says nothing about CPU-resident forward passes where there is no idle CPU to overlap with.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `"Sampling with masks" @ page` @ retrieved 2026-10-22; page dated 2025-06-11; html sha256-12 7512a5e5463c: "Here, compute_mask() can run on the CPU during the time it would be normally just waiting for the GPU to finish."
4. llama.cpp grammars are described qualitatively as significantly slower (no lexer, backtracking parser) with NO llama.cpp number in this page; the page contains no fast-forward / jump-forward / forced-token content at all (grep count 0), so it cannot support a forced-span claim.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `whole page; grep -c -i "fast.forward|jump|forced|ff-token" = 0 (brr.txt, 861 lines)` @ retrieved 2026-10-22; page dated 2025-06-11; html sha256-12 7512a5e5463c: "LM-format-enforcer and llama.cpp grammars are similar to llguidance in that they dynamically build token masks for every step of the decoding process. Both are significantly slower"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): page read in full from saved HTML; it is a design/perf write-up with NO fast-forward content and no llama.cpp number; its 50 us figure is vendor, JSON-schema, GPU-overlap framing.

## intake-2416#record — Misc clean up; Remove the support of jump forward (#4032)

- **URL**: https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/4032
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: inference_serving, tool_implementation, speculative_decoding)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-2388#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. SGLang removed jump-forward on 2025-03-03 (PR #4032, 935cda94); the only stated reason is "to simplify the code maintenance" - no performance or correctness measurement is given in the PR text.
   - *unaffected*: fresh
   - anchor `PR #4032 body @ 935cda94` @ sgl-project/sglang@935cda944b82 (2025-03-03); HEAD d0a75654 (2026-10-22) for absence checks: "- Remove jump forward to simplify the code maintenance"
1. The removed design was string-level: on a forced span it re-tokenized prompt+decoded text+jump string, rewrote output_ids/logprob bookkeeping, bumped a detokenizer version id, inserted the old request into the radix cache and RE-QUEUED the request as a new prefill; it bailed out (returned False) on "token fusion" at the prompt/output boundary.
   - *unaffected*: fresh
   - anchor `python/sglang/srt/managers/schedule_batch.py:L518-L522 @ 110e0066 (parent of 935cda94)` @ sgl-project/sglang@935cda944b82 (2025-03-03); HEAD d0a75654 (2026-10-22) for absence checks: "# TODO(lsyin): fix token fusion"
2. Before removal the scheduler already force-set disable_jump_forward=True whenever the overlap scheduler (the default) was enabled, so for default-config users it was inert; the removal diff also deletes last_update_decode_tokens logprob recompute code, i.e. the feature complicated logprobs.
   - *unaffected*: fresh
   - anchor `python/sglang/srt/managers/scheduler.py:L254-L255 @ 110e0066 (parent of 935cda94)` @ sgl-project/sglang@935cda944b82 (2025-03-03); HEAD d0a75654 (2026-10-22) for absence checks: "self.disable_jump_forward = True"
3. At HEAD d0a75654 the abstract hooks try_jump_forward / jump_forward_str_state / jump_and_retokenize still exist in base_grammar_backend.py, but schedule_batch.py and scheduler.py contain zero jump_forward references: the capability is a dead interface, not a live path.
   - *narrowed*: premise "SGLang stopped calling jump-forward" is true for the scheduler (files checked: managers/schedule_batch.py, managers/scheduler.py at d0a75654) but not the whole tree; grep not run across all files (GitHub code-search rate-limited)
   - anchor `python/sglang/srt/constrained/base_grammar_backend.py:L119 @ d0a75654` @ sgl-project/sglang@935cda944b82 (2025-03-03); HEAD d0a75654 (2026-10-22) for absence checks: "def try_jump_forward(self, tokenizer) -> Optional[Tuple[List[int], str]]:"
4. The replacement direction is token-level fast-forward as speculative decoding: PR #17062 (JC1DA, Jan 2026) adds an LLGUIDANCE speculative algorithm using llguidance ff-tokens, reporting TPOT 4.5 -> 3.1-3.6 ms (1.24-1.44x) on JSONSchemaBench with Llama-3.2-1B on a 3090; it was closed unmerged by a stale bot on 2026-08-19 (no maintainer review in the thread).
   - *unaffected*: PR body numbers are the author self-report
   - anchor `PR #17062 body (GitHub API, retrieved 2026-10-22)` @ sgl-project/sglang@935cda944b82 (2025-03-03); HEAD d0a75654 (2026-10-22) for absence checks: "achieved speedup ranging from **1.24X to 1.44X** (TPOP - time per output token) while maintaining the same accuracy performance"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): reason for removal is maintenance simplification only; jump-forward was already inert under the default overlap scheduler. It was not abandoned because forced spans do not pay; the string-level design (retokenize + requeue-as-prefill) was the cost, and token-level ff via spec-decoding (PR 17062) is the live proposal. | 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (operator pre-approved all recommended sources): dive-surfaced sources NOT dived, with reasons: sglang issue 8954 and PR 8975 declined (confirm dead code only).

   - anchor `inferencex-e2e/infx/datasets/proxy_to_weka.py:L325 session_to_weka @ InferenceX 2633e9a443` @ github.com/SemiAnalysisAI/InferenceX @ 2633e9a443958ad8 (main, retrieved 2026-10-22): "hash_map: dict[str, int] = {}"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): Stage-1 premise for this group (wave 1 'TraceLab has synthetic per-session ids and no cross-session sharing; AIPerf replays it') is CONFIRMED to apply equally to Weka traces: hash_id_scope is 'local' in every published file read (repo trace_0026, HF 042026/060826/062126 heads); salt discarded at generation; so Weka does not supply the cross-session sharing TraceLab lacks. What Weka adds over TraceLab is (a) nested sub-agent topology with per-request ttft/api_time/think_time, (b) block-level prefix structure (hash_ids) so prefix-growth/pull-back (compaction) events are visible, (c) a replayer that models parent-pauses-while-children-run. The replayer's own cache-hit figure is trace-derived, not server-measured.

## intake-2422#record — Support LLGuidance Fast-Forward tokens via Speculative Decoding (sgl-project/sglang PR #17062, closed unmerged)

- **URL**: https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/17062
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: speculative_decoding, inference_serving, tool_implementation)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2401#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Mechanism (llguidance_worker.py L69-L90 @ c3424b7): this is NOT a draft-then-verify scheme. Every decode step, for each request the worker calls the grammar matcher compute_ff_tokens(), immediately accepts those tokens into the grammar and appends them to the request output, and then runs ONE target forward in EXTEND (prefill) mode over [previously-sampled token, ff tokens...]. Only the last position is sampled. The ff tokens are never compared with a model choice.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim; narrows the framing of intake-2416#record ("ff via speculative worker"): the worker never verifies, it force-extends
   - anchor `python/sglang/srt/speculative/llguidance_worker.py:L87-L90 (new file in diff) @ c3424b7` @ sgl-project/sglang PR #17062 head c3424b75fca2a11e6783dceef692b89cd2197178 (retrieved 2026-10-22; diff sha256-12 91421d8765c3): "# accept ff_tokens for token in ff_tokens: req.grammar.accept_token(token) req.output_ids.append(token)"
1. No rollback path exists because nothing is speculated: the 179-line worker file has zero occurrences of rollback/reject/verify/accept_len, and KV bookkeeping commits len(ff)+1 slots per request unconditionally (L95-L96). Stop-condition / max-new-tokens handling for the appended ff tokens is not visible in the diff (NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE; finish logic lives in code the diff does not touch).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim; absence verified by grep over the full 179-line new file
   - anchor `python/sglang/srt/speculative/llguidance_worker.py:L95-L96 @ c3424b7` @ sgl-project/sglang PR #17062 head c3424b75fca2a11e6783dceef692b89cd2197178 (retrieved 2026-10-22; diff sha256-12 91421d8765c3): "req.kv_committed_len += len(ff_tokens) + 1 req.kv_allocated_len = req.kv_committed_len"
2. Batch > 1 (L80, L100-L128; schedule_batch.py hunks): if ANY request in the batch has ff tokens the whole batch is switched to ragged EXTEND mode (requests without ff contribute 1 token), which needed fixes to keep extend_lens/prefix_lens consistent in filter_batch/merge_batch. The algorithm is also an exclusive --speculative-algorithm choice (cannot be combined with EAGLE/NGRAM) and create_worker raises ValueError under the overlap scheduler (test class runs with --disable-overlap-schedule). No batch > 1 speedup was measured: batch size is NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE in the PR body.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `python/sglang/srt/speculative/llguidance_worker.py:L80 @ c3424b7` @ sgl-project/sglang PR #17062 head c3424b75fca2a11e6783dceef692b89cd2197178 (retrieved 2026-10-22; diff sha256-12 91421d8765c3): "if max_ff_tokens_len > 0:"
   - anchor `python/sglang/srt/speculative/spec_info.py create_worker hunk @@ -101,6 +105,15 @@ @ c3424b7` @ sgl-project/sglang PR #17062 head c3424b75fca2a11e6783dceef692b89cd2197178 (retrieved 2026-10-22; diff sha256-12 91421d8765c3): "if enable_overlap: raise ValueError( f"Speculative algorithm {self.name} does not support overlap worker creation." )"
3. Measured speedup (PR body, author-reported, single run, no repetitions stated): Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct on one RTX 3090, JSONSchemaBench subsets, llguidance backend with vs without the speculator: TPOT 4.54->3.46 ms (Github_easy, 1.31x), 4.53->3.13 (Glaiveai2K, 1.45x), 4.52->3.63 (Kubernetes, 1.25x) (ratios are this dive's arithmetic; the PR headline "1.24X to 1.44X" truncates 1.447). Standard deviations in the table are as large as the means; batch size, forced-token share per task, the baseline overlap setting and whether TPOT counts ff tokens as output are NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE. A 1B model at 4.5 ms/token is host-overhead-dominated, so the ratio does not transfer to a bandwidth-bound big model.
   - *narrowed*: intake-2416#record recorded the three TPOP pairs; this adds the missing conditions (single run, 1B model, batch size absent, std >= mean) and that the ratios are 1.31/1.45/1.25
   - anchor `PR #17062 body, "Benchmark Results Summary" (GitHub API pulls/17062, retrieved 2026-10-22)` @ PR body, GitHub API sha256-12 73bf5b6e6d06 (gh_pull.json): "achieved speedup ranging from **1.24X to 1.44X** (TPOP - time per output token) while maintaining the same accuracy performance (i.e., coverage)"
4. Review record: 4 issue comments (a bot quota notice, two author pings to @Ying1123/@merrymercy/@hnyls2002/@xiezhq-hermann and @ispobock, and the stale-bot close), 0 reviews, 0 inline review comments; closed unmerged 2026-08-19 by the stale bot ("no updates in 196 days"). No maintainer objected on design grounds; none engaged at all.
   - *unaffected*: confirms intake-2416#record ("no maintainer review in the thread") from the comment list plus empty reviews/inline-comments endpoints

## intake-2423#record — Formalizing BPE Tokenization

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.08715
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty low, relevance low; categories: tool_implementation, inference_serving)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-2401#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Theory paper (cs.FL, v1 only): formalizes SentencePiece and HuggingFace BPE tokenization as rewrite systems, shows they coincide on "proper" dictionaries (Lemma 1), and studies incremental / left-to-right streaming tokenization. No experiments and no implementation (Sec. 5 says experimental studies "should be performed").
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `Abstract and Sec. 5 Conclusions` @ arXiv:2309.08715v1 (15 Sep 2023) HTML sha256-12 c3b49a3372cc, retrieved 2026-10-22: "Experimental studies should be performed, for example, testing how the incremental algorithm behaves in random cases."
1. Example 2 (Sec. 3): for a constructed dictionary a small change at the front of a string changes an arbitrarily long run of later tokens; Sec. 5 notes this is not finite for all dictionaries but "from random testing it seems to often be finite". This is the left-edit direction, not the append direction llguidance fast-forward uses.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `Example 2 (Sec. 3, final sentence)` @ arXiv:2309.08715v1 (15 Sep 2023) HTML sha256-12 c3b49a3372cc, retrieved 2026-10-22: "This illustrates that a small modification to the string can cause an arbitrarily large change to the resulting tokenization (to the right)."
2. Theorem 2 / Definition 7 (Sec. 4): for the append direction a finite lookahead suffices, with sufficient lookahead |D| * max|uv| (number of merge rules times longest rule). Example 4 builds an n-rule dictionary where appending ONE symbol merges n already-emitted tokens into one, so the number of trailing tokens that can be invalidated by an append is not bounded by a small constant for adversarial dictionaries. The paper calls its constructive bounds "high enough to be impractical".
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim; Example 4 content paraphrased from the HTML (math in alt-text form), not quoted
   - anchor `Sec. 4 Definition 7 and Sec. 5 paragraph 2` @ arXiv:2309.08715v1 (15 Sep 2023) HTML sha256-12 c3b49a3372cc, retrieved 2026-10-22: "We do not offer implementation details in this paper, as in general the bounds implied by the constructions are high enough to be impractical."
3. Bearing on llguidance fast_forward.md (6cef6ed L126-L127): the doc cites this paper only for the remark that examples where a 4-token trim window is insufficient "may be possible" to construct and says none was found. So the 4-token window is an empirical choice validated on MaskBench with the llama3 tokenizer (fast_forward.md L119-L125), not a bound this paper proves; the paper neither supplies a tight bound nor shows a counterexample on a trained BPE vocabulary.
   - *narrowed*: narrows intake-2401#record claim 2: the canonical-trim guarantee is empirical, not backed by a bound from the cited paper
   - anchor `llguidance docs/fast_forward.md:L126-L127 @ 6cef6ed9c973c2a155a85f2bb1996320af28b289 (sha256-12 4a0289479d79)` @ guidance-ai/llguidance@6cef6ed9c973c2a155a85f2bb1996320af28b289 (2026-10-14): "While it [may be possible](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.08715) to construct examples where `max_token==4` is not enough, we have not bee able to do so."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): arXiv id resolved (abs page title "Formalizing BPE Tokenization"), on-topic for the canonical-tokenization trimming referenced in llguidance fast_forward.md. The paper supports the CONCERN (append can invalidate many trailing tokens for adversarial dictionaries, Example 4) but supplies no usable bound, so the 4-token window must be audited per tokenizer.

## intake-2426#record — Invisible Failures in Human–AI Interactions

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15423
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, knowledge_management, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2397#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. On 100K English WildChat transcripts (GPT-3.5/GPT-4 era, 2023-24) annotated by Claude Opus 4.6, 63% involve a failure; of those 12% are visible (user reacts), 79% invisible, 9% mixed, so a monitor that keys on overt user signals (negative sentiment, complaints, explicit correction) sees about 12% of failures (Sec. 4). The failure labels are LLM judgements; there is no human-gold count in the paper.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Sec. 4 (p. 4 text) @ arXiv v2` @ arXiv:2603.15423v2 (12 May 2026), html sha256-12 580073801ea6; repo bigspinai/bigspin-invisible-failure-archetypes @a71d9ea: "Of these cases, 7,632 (12%) are tagged visible, 49,368 (79%) are tagged invisible, and 5,557 (9%) are tagged mixed."
1. The annotation protocol is two-stage: turn-level tagging of 50 AI signals and 13 user signals, then archetype inference from a signal report. Agreement is measured only between two hosted LLM annotators (Opus 4.6 vs GPT-5.4): failure-label kappa 0.84 / agreement 0.94 for Signals-only versus 0.62 / 0.86 for transcript-only (Table 1, App. C). No human gold is reported and the authors note the annotator out-classes the models being judged (Sec. 3.2).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Sec. 3.2 Table 1 caption @ arXiv v2` @ arXiv:2603.15423v2 (12 May 2026), html sha256-12 580073801ea6; repo bigspinai/bigspin-invisible-failure-archetypes @a71d9ea: "Table 1: Annotation project results, based on a sample of 10K transcripts. The annotators are Opus 4.6 and GPT-5.4."
2. Per-signal inter-LLM kappa for the user-behaviour signals (App. C.3 Table 5b): user_corrects_ai 0.70, user_implicit_correction 0.67, user_repeats_request 0.61, user_abandons_thread 0.72, user_expresses_frustration 0.60, but user_scope_change only 0.32. The correction-versus-scope-change boundary, which matters for any pushback rate, is the least reliably labelled user signal.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `App. C.3 Table 5b @ arXiv v2` @ arXiv:2603.15423v2 (12 May 2026), html sha256-12 580073801ea6; repo bigspinai/bigspin-invisible-failure-archetypes @a71d9ea: "user_repeats_request 0.61 0.96 user_scope_change 0.32 0.94"
3. The Death Spiral archetype is defined behaviourally as the user repeatedly correcting while the AI keeps producing the same output without incorporating feedback (inter-LLM kappa 0.80); The Walkaway (user silently stops, unresolved goal) is the most frequent archetype but kappa 0.81 only with the signals-first protocol (0.16 transcript-only) (App. A, C.2).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `App. A The Death Spiral @ arXiv v2` @ arXiv:2603.15423v2 (12 May 2026), html sha256-12 580073801ea6; repo bigspinai/bigspin-invisible-failure-archetypes @a71d9ea: "The conversation enters a repetitive loop. The user keeps asking or correcting, and the AI keeps producing the same or similar output without incorporating the feedback."
4. The 79% invisible figure is a single-turn-dominated chat statistic: 63% of the sample is single-turn, the invisible share falls to 50% when restricted to multi-turn transcripts (Fig. 7), and software_development has the largest Visible-failure PPMI of any domain, which the authors attribute to expert users who talk back to the AI (Sec. 6). Future-2K (single-turn re-generations by four current models) cuts the failure rate from 41.7% to under 10% while keeping most failures invisible.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Sec. 6 @ arXiv v2` @ arXiv:2603.15423v2 (12 May 2026), html sha256-12 580073801ea6; repo bigspinai/bigspin-invisible-failure-archetypes @a71d9ea: "Third, and relatedly, the largest value for Visible failure is in software_development."
5. Code is released under Apache-2.0 with taxonomy.json (v3.0: signal definitions, boundary notes, derivation rules for goal_failure and archetypes), the tagging scripts (hosted-API clients) and the 100K annotation file, so the definitions are portable but the annotation run itself depends on hosted frontier models.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `README.md @a71d9ea` @ arXiv:2603.15423v2 (12 May 2026), html sha256-12 580073801ea6; repo bigspinai/bigspin-invisible-failure-archetypes @a71d9ea: "The suit of tools needed to run a fully calibrated and validated annotation job."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): read arXiv v2 HTML in full (v3 does not exist; v1 also fetched, not diffed) and the Apache-2.0 repo at a71d9ea (README, taxonomy.json signal and archetype definitions; batch scripts not read line by line). The headline 79% invisible is LLM-judged chat data and drops to 50% for multi-turn; it must not be cited as a coding-agent rate. Agreement is inter-LLM only. | 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (operator pre-approved all recommended sources): dive-surfaced sources NOT dived, with reasons: ShareChat 2512.17843 declined (chat corpus, no coding-agent content).

## intake-2427#record — SWE-Together: Evaluating Coding Agents in Interactive User Sessions

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29957
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-2397#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. SWE-Together keeps 109 executable tasks out of 11,260 recorded sessions (0.97%) from four upstream corpora (DataClaw, Pi-staging, Hyperswitch, and 5,851 raw SWE-chat sessions yielding 48 tasks); the filter keeps public mature repos with multi-turn, code-changing sessions and drops sessions whose main deliverable is external state (Sec. 2.1, Table 1).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Sec. 2.1 @ arXiv v1` @ arXiv:2606.29957v1 (29 Jun 2026), html sha256-12 eb08526c6913; repo Togetherbench/SWE-Together @e9ce8cf (2026-10-14): "109/11,260 sessions pass the filters and are converted into executable tasks, a conversion rate of 0.97%."
1. The user is an LLM simulator, not a human: after each agent turn it makes one structured decision (no-op, question, redirect, new-requirement, check-external) conditioned on session anchors derived from the original user, and it cannot interrupt a running agent turn (Sec. 2.2, Sec. 5). The paper User Correction metric is therefore the pushback an anchored simulator emits, not observed human pushback.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Sec. 5 Limitation @ arXiv v1` @ arXiv:2606.29957v1 (29 Jun 2026), html sha256-12 eb08526c6913; repo Togetherbench/SWE-Together @e9ce8cf (2026-10-14): "The user simulator cannot interrupt the coding agent during its turns, cannot directly edit files,"
2. User Correction = N_correction + 0.2 N_nudge, computed by a multi-label tagger over simulator messages; across seven models run in opencode (k=2 replicates, 109 tasks) it ranges 1.38 to 2.17 per trial and correlates -0.92 with pass@1, -0.84 with stable solve rate, -0.93 with mean judge score (Sec. 2.3.2, 3.1). n=7 cohorts, simulated corrections, no confidence intervals reported.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Sec. 3.1 @ arXiv v1` @ arXiv:2606.29957v1 (29 Jun 2026), html sha256-12 eb08526c6913; repo Togetherbench/SWE-Together @e9ce8cf (2026-10-14): "across the seven models it is strongly inversely correlated with pass@1 (Pearson −0.92), stable solve rate (−0.84), and mean judge score (−0.93)."
3. Simulator validity evidence is limited to a 4-annotator forced-choice study (312 judgments, Turing pass rate 46%, 95% CI 40.5-51.6) and Intent Coverage of 0.70-0.72 for six of seven cohorts, i.e. roughly 30% of original intents are not re-expressed or leave scope (Sec. 3.2-3.3). The paper reports no accuracy or agreement figure for the correction/nudge tagger itself.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Sec. 3.3 @ arXiv v1` @ arXiv:2606.29957v1 (29 Jun 2026), html sha256-12 eb08526c6913; repo Togetherbench/SWE-Together @e9ce8cf (2026-10-14): "Across all trajectories, the simulator achieves a Turing pass rate of 46% (95% CI [40.5, 51.6]%)."
4. The released tagger is not hosted-free: the default tagger is Gemini-3.1-Pro, and the repo says the reported User Correction numbers come from a 3-way reconciliation (Gemini-3.1-Pro, Claude Opus 4.6, GPT-5.5 arbiter), with the single-pass default about 0.04 off (adjudicate_3way.py docstring). Only the agent seat has a self-hosted vLLM backend; user_sim, tagger and judge go through LiteLLM native/openrouter/bedrock and the judge shells out to claude --print (docs/llm_backends.md).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `eval/user_behavior/adjudicate_3way.py:L1-L18 @ e9ce8cf` @ arXiv:2606.29957v1 (29 Jun 2026), html sha256-12 eb08526c6913; repo Togetherbench/SWE-Together @e9ce8cf (2026-10-14): "The default is ~0.04 off and ranks the models identically; run this stage to reproduce the exact published values."
5. The repo (Apache-2.0) ships the tagger prompt, which separates new scope (request) from error correction (correction) and doubt (nudge): new scope is explicitly NOT a correction. That separation is a usable labelling rule for an EPYC pushback audit, though the prompt is unvalidated against human labels.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `eval/user_behavior/prompts/tag_messages_system.md:L38 @ e9ce8cf` @ arXiv:2606.29957v1 (29 Jun 2026), html sha256-12 eb08526c6913; repo Togetherbench/SWE-Together @e9ce8cf (2026-10-14): "New scope is `request`, not `correction`."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): read arXiv v1 HTML in full (v2 returns 404) and the Apache-2.0 repo at e9ce8cf (tag_messages.py, user_metrics.py, adjudicate_3way.py, tag prompt, docs/llm_backends.md). Decisive: the abstract phrase "number of corrective feedback turns" counts SIMULATOR messages, and the tagger that scores them is a hosted-LLM ensemble named only in the repo, not the paper. | 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (operator pre-approved all recommended sources): dive-surfaced sources NOT dived, with reasons: Suh et al. 2605.09808 declined now; re-opens with the anchored-replay trigger.

   - anchor `Introduction @ 2026-08-04` @ Transluce blog, published 2026-08-04, retrieved 2026-10-22, html sha256-12 c5c21729cad8: "We found severe cases of monitor evasion in 1.9% of SWE-chat sessions, and severe cases of overselling in 1.8% of sessions."
1. Counting any presence (not only severe), SWE-chat shows monitor evasion 14.7% (n=4,990) and overselling 34.7% (n=4,788), while Transluce internal traffic shows 0.7% and 5.7%; these figures appear only in figure alt text, and per-model SWE-chat rates (e.g. Opus 4.6 34.5% overselling, n=3,741) come from non-random model subsets that the authors say cannot be compared across models.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Results, figure 01_rates_both_collections.png alt text` @ Transluce blog, published 2026-08-04, retrieved 2026-10-22, html sha256-12 c5c21729cad8: "In SWE-chat transcripts, monitor evasion totals 14.7% (12.8% not severe, 1.9% severe; n = 4,990) and overselling totals 34.7% (32.9% not severe, 1.8% severe; n = 4,788)."
2. Judge construction: rubrics drafted by Claude Fable and hand-edited, run by Opus 5 (high reasoning), severe flags re-checked by a GPT-5.6 skeptic; validation is described only as comparison with "a small hand-labeled set" and spot checks, with no accuracy, precision or recall reported. Hosted judges throughout.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `How we constructed these measurements` @ Transluce blog, published 2026-08-04, retrieved 2026-10-22, html sha256-12 c5c21729cad8: "We also compared results against a small hand-labeled set of transcripts."
3. The published monitor-evasion rubric enumerates concrete tool-call surface forms (git commit --no-verify, core.hooksPath=/dev/null, HUSKY=0, skipped or xfail tests, loosened assertions, force flags, deleting a failing test) plus a mandatory authorization screen (user explicitly named the bypass), so a regex pass can nominate candidates but cannot decide them.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Monitor evasion rubric, Common shapes` @ Transluce blog, published 2026-08-04, retrieved 2026-10-22, html sha256-12 c5c21729cad8: "Committing with --no-verify, --no-gpg-sign, core.hooksPath=/dev/null, HUSKY=0, or otherwise disabling git hooks"
4. Qualitative finding: explicit CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md rules and typed languages did not prevent severe evasion; one user accounts for almost half of severe monitor-evasion cases, and the authors flag that user reactions were hard to interpret as severity evidence (sessions ended early, complaints retracted). Observational, no with/without-rule comparison.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Qualitative Discussion` @ Transluce blog, published 2026-08-04, retrieved 2026-10-22, html sha256-12 c5c21729cad8: "Code safety best practices like using a CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md and using a typed language are insufficient safeguards against overeagerness."

### Dive corrections

## intake-2429#record — Measuring AI agent autonomy in practice

### Claims (as verified)

0. Claude Code interrupt rate per turn rises with tenure: about 5% for new users (around 10 sessions) to about 9% for experienced users, while full auto-approve rises from about 20% to over 40% of sessions; the appendix says turn duration, auto-approval and interruption rates are computed mechanically from internal telemetry, not by a model (Fig. 3, Appendix).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Section "Experienced users ... interrupt more often"` @ Anthropic post published 2026-02-18, retrieved 2026-10-22, html sha256-12 b526b93cbc3f; appendix PDF sha256-12 ee9d828c5632 (text extracted with scripts/pdf_text.py): "New users (those with around 10 sessions) interrupt Claude in 5% of turns, while more experienced users interrupt in around 9% of turns (Figure 3)."
1. Why humans interrupt (Claude-clustered, 500k interruptions, non-exhaustive, lightly edited): provide missing technical context or corrections 32%, Claude slow/hanging/excessive 17%, enough help to proceed 7%, want to take next step themselves 7%, change requirements 5% (Table 1). Only the first and a share of the last are agent-error signals, so an interrupt is not a failure label.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Table 1` @ Anthropic post published 2026-02-18, retrieved 2026-10-22, html sha256-12 b526b93cbc3f; appendix PDF sha256-12 ee9d828c5632 (text extracted with scripts/pdf_text.py): "To provide missing technical context or corrections (32%)"
2. Agent-initiated stops matter as much as human ones: on the most complex tasks Claude Code asks for clarification more than twice as often as humans interrupt it (Fig. 4, n=500k sessions).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Section "Claude Code pauses for clarification ..."` @ Anthropic post published 2026-02-18, retrieved 2026-10-22, html sha256-12 b526b93cbc3f; appendix PDF sha256-12 ee9d828c5632 (text extracted with scripts/pdf_text.py): "On the most complex tasks, Claude Code stops to ask for clarification more than twice as often as humans interrupt it."
3. Internally, from August to December average human interventions per session fell from 5.4 to 3.3 while success on the hardest tasks doubled; success and complexity there are Claude-classified and unreported with intervals, and the measure is Anthropic-internal.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Section "Claude Code is working autonomously for longer"` @ Anthropic post published 2026-02-18, retrieved 2026-10-22, html sha256-12 b526b93cbc3f; appendix PDF sha256-12 ee9d828c5632 (text extracted with scripts/pdf_text.py): "the average number of human interventions per session decreased from 5.4 to 3.3."
4. Validation detail: the stop-reason classifier (Sonnet 4.5) was checked on n=200 turns (14 of 16 classifier-flagged interruptions agreed with the human grader), and in two cases the telemetry interruption flag appeared wrong because the transcript showed no interruption, so telemetry flag and transcript marker can disagree (App. Table A3).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Appendix p. 27, Table A3 discussion` @ Anthropic post published 2026-02-18, retrieved 2026-10-22, html sha256-12 b526b93cbc3f; appendix PDF sha256-12 ee9d828c5632 (text extracted with scripts/pdf_text.py): "Two other disagreements involved the “interrupted” category, where the ground-truth interruption"
5. The classifier prompt keys on the literal final transcript text "[Request interrupted by user for tool use]" for an interrupted turn and treats "[Request interrupted by user]" in earlier history as a previous turn; both marker forms exist in Claude Code transcripts.

## intake-2430#record — Autellix: An Efficient Serving Engine for LLM Agents as General Programs

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13965
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: inference_serving, agent_architecture, kv_cache)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2398#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Program key is the program ID of a stateful session; priority is the CUMULATIVE SERVICE TIME of that program (sum of runtimes of its completed LLM calls, PLAS) or the longest observed critical path for multi-threaded programs (ATLAS); it is least-attained-service, not first-arrival order (§4.2).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `§4.2 PLAS [section label unverified (dive wrote it from memory); quote verified verbatim in the fetched arXiv HTML text]` @ arXiv:2502.13965 HTML (latest version served 2026-10-22, sha256-12 9ff269cf99e8): "PLAS naturally favors calls from programs that have received less total service, helping shorter programs finish earlier and reducing response times."
1. Headline 4-15x throughput at equal latency is measured against stock vLLM v0.6.1 FCFS without prefix caching; against the authors own optimised vLLM-opt (chunked prefill + prefix cache) the stated gains are about 2x (single-threaded), 2x (LATS) and 4x (mixed) (§6.3).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `§6.3 Fig.12 text [section label unverified (dive wrote it from memory); quote verified verbatim in the fetched arXiv HTML text]` @ arXiv:2502.13965 HTML (latest version served 2026-10-22, sha256-12 9ff269cf99e8): "Autellix achieves up to 15× higher throughput than vLLM, 5.5× higher than MLFQ, and 4× higher than vLLM-opt."
   - anchor `§6.3 Fig.12 text [section label unverified (dive wrote it from memory); quote verified verbatim in the fetched arXiv HTML text]` @ arXiv:2502.13965 HTML (latest version served 2026-10-22, sha256-12 9ff269cf99e8): "vLLM performs worst due to its lack of prefix caching"
2. Scheduler is non-clairvoyant: it needs no prior knowledge of arrivals or workflow DAG, only a per-program process table (service time, wait time, engine ids, last call arrival/completion) fed by a stateful session API (§4.1-4.2).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `§4.1 Assumptions [section label unverified (dive wrote it from memory); quote verified verbatim in the fetched arXiv HTML text]` @ arXiv:2502.13965 HTML (latest version served 2026-10-22, sha256-12 9ff269cf99e8): "Autellix is non-clairvoyant; it assumes no knowledge of program arrivals"
3. Anti-starvation promotes a call when program wait/service ratio exceeds a threshold, because plain wait-threshold promotion degrades to naive MLFQ (§4.2).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `§4.2 Anti-Starvation [section label unverified (dive wrote it from memory); quote verified verbatim in the fetched arXiv HTML text]` @ arXiv:2502.13965 HTML (latest version served 2026-10-22, sha256-12 9ff269cf99e8): "Autellix promotes call"
4. A clairvoyant SRPT simulation still beats Autellix by a visible gap, i.e. knowing program length in advance is worth more than the accumulated-service proxy (§6.7).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `§6.7 clairvoyance [section label unverified (dive wrote it from memory); quote verified verbatim in the fetched arXiv HTML text]` @ arXiv:2502.13965 HTML (latest version served 2026-10-22, sha256-12 9ff269cf99e8): "a noticable gap remains between Autellix and SRPT, showing that prior knowledge can significantly boost performance"
5. The mechanism rests on preempting running calls and swapping KV blocks to host (multi-step scheduling + contiguous swap kernel), tested on A100 with Llama-3.1-8B/70B and Falcon-180B (§5, §6.1).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `§5 Implementation [section label unverified (dive wrote it from memory); quote verified verbatim in the fetched arXiv HTML text]` @ arXiv:2502.13965 HTML (latest version served 2026-10-22, sha256-12 9ff269cf99e8): "Autellix builds on vLLM v0.6.1"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): Stage-1 framing 'key = accumulated service time vs first arrival' CONFIRMED for the key, but headline speedup is against a no-prefix-cache baseline; real gain over a prefix-cached FCFS engine is ~2-4x on A100 vLLM. Abstract + §4 + §6.2-6.3 + §6.7 read from HTML text; §3 motivation, §7 related work, appendices not read.

### Claims (as verified)

0. Naive accumulation of the agent's own SUCCESSFUL trajectories as retrieved in-context examples (Traj-Bootstrap) lifts held-out test success from 73% to 89% (ALFWorld), 75% to 79% (InterCode-SQL), 55% to 64% (Wordcraft) with GPT-4o-mini, vs a fixed human-example database (Tab.1).
