1. Protocol: strict train/test task separation; databases built only from training tasks, evaluated on unseen test tasks; 5 trials per method, mean with spread reported (§6, App. F.2).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `App. F.2 [section label unverified (dive wrote it from memory); quote verified verbatim in the fetched arXiv HTML text]` @ arXiv:2505.00234 HTML (retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 f1743eed3891): "evaluation conducted on a separate set of test tasks never seen during the database construction phase"
2. Population-style database curation (+DB) and per-task best-exemplar selection by empirical retrieval utility (+Exemplar) add further gains: best 0.93 ALFWorld, 0.82 InterCode-SQL; +Exemplar best on Wordcraft (0.72) (Tab.1).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `§6 Tab.1 text [section label unverified (dive wrote it from memory); quote verified verbatim in the fetched arXiv HTML text]` @ arXiv:2505.00234 HTML (retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 f1743eed3891): "achieves the best final task success rates on both ALFWorld and InterCode-SQL (0.93 and 0.82 respectively)"
3. Gains are noisy across trials (databases of identical procedure differ markedly) and show diminishing returns: most gain within first 25% of added training tasks (Fig.1-2).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `§6 Fig.2 text [section label unverified (dive wrote it from memory); quote verified verbatim in the fetched arXiv HTML text]` @ arXiv:2505.00234 HTML (retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 f1743eed3891): "most gains occur within the first 25% of added training tasks"
4. Cost: authors state test-time compute is identical across methods, curation costs multiples of training-time inference; per-step top-k retrieval of whole trajectories into the prompt is not token-quantified (§5.4) -- NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE for prompt-token overhead.

5. Equivalent to ~pass@2-3 of the fixed-DB agent and comparable to upgrading gpt-4o-mini to gpt-4o on ALFWorld (Tab.2-3); examples are full trajectories, not distilled lessons.

## intake-2433#record — Evaluating management of KV Cache within an inference system

- **URL**: https://medium.com/@callan.j.fox/evaluating-management-of-kv-cache-within-an-inference-system-2d7c3d266c3a
- **Verification**: dive-verified

### Claims (as verified)

0. Three-stage methodology: (1) single-prompt TTFT at 0% vs 100% cache hit as smoke test; (2) overhead test: working set sized to FIT in HBM KV so an external KV manager only handles demotions, compared against prefix-only baseline with identical commands; (3) tier-isolation: working set sized to ~80% of external tier capacity above HBM, deterministic FIFO cycling so reuse hits the external tier.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `Overhead test section` @ Medium RSS feed full-content (feed fetched 2026-10-22, sha256-12 43d80667086a; text sha256-12 ff9f6b63edf3): "There should be no difference in performance at all, otherwise there is a penalty just for sending KV Cache data to the external KV Manager"
   - anchor `Tier isolation section` @ Medium RSS feed full-content (feed fetched 2026-10-22, sha256-12 43d80667086a; text sha256-12 ff9f6b63edf3): "set the working set size to 80% of that number"
1. cache_rate_tester ramps concurrency per cache-hit rate (0/25/50/75/90/100%) up to a TTFT ceiling (--max-ttft 10 avg) and reinitialises the prompt pool per rate; a working_set_tester additionally grows the working set to expose each tier boundary.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `Overhead test cmd` @ Medium RSS feed full-content (feed fetched 2026-10-22, sha256-12 43d80667086a; text sha256-12 ff9f6b63edf3): "--cache-hit-rates 0 25 50 75 90 100"
2. Author states run-to-run variance exists and the tool retries at peak concurrency; no repeat counts or confidence intervals are given (NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `Tier isolation section` @ Medium RSS feed full-content (feed fetched 2026-10-22, sha256-12 43d80667086a; text sha256-12 ff9f6b63edf3): "there is some variance in each of the test runs"
3. No results table: only illustrative graphs (not machine-readable in the text); setup is 8xH100, vLLM + LMCache, Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B-FP8 and Llama-3.3-70B-FP8. Vendor (WEKA) author.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `Test environment` @ Medium RSS feed full-content (feed fetched 2026-10-22, sha256-12 43d80667086a; text sha256-12 ff9f6b63edf3): "I will be testing with a single DGX with 8x H100 GPUs"
4. Sizing rule: compute per-token KV bytes (48 KB/token for the 30B-A3B FP8 model) and set working set vs reported GPU KV capacity (5,570,368 tokens) to force or avoid overflow.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `Overhead test section` @ Medium RSS feed full-content (feed fetched 2026-10-22, sha256-12 43d80667086a; text sha256-12 ff9f6b63edf3): "each token is 48 KB in size for this model"

0. MIT-licensed (LICENSE, Callan Fox 2025) generator behind the kv-cache-tester traces: joins claude-code-proxy request captures (requests.db) with Claude Code's local JSONL transcripts; emitted traces carry block hashes, never message text. BUT the repo itself ships a content-bearing worked example (examples/jsonl: 1 parent + 4 sub-agent transcripts, 1.3 MB parent; examples/requests.db 13 MB) so the raw Claude Code JSONL and proxy-DB schemas are readable first-hand.
   - *narrowed*: Repo is a generator; the stage-1 note that traces carry no text is right for outputs but the repo's examples/ DO carry real transcripts and a proxy DB.
   - anchor `ev/aca/LICENSE:L1 @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14)` @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14): "MIT License"
   - anchor `README.md:L167 @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14)` @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14): "Traces are compact JSON files capturing cache block structure without actual message content."
   - anchor `README.md:L47 @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14)` @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14): "- `examples/jsonl/` — JSONL conversation files from Claude Code's local storage (1 parent + 4 sub-agent files)"
1. Exact tokenisation/normalisation (build_minimal_traces.py MinimalTraceBuilder, kv_common.py): per request the three sections tools, system, messages are each normalised (recursive drop of any dict key named cache_control or signature), serialised with json.dumps(separators=(',',':')) WITHOUT sort_keys, and encoded separately with tiktoken gpt-4 (disallowed_special=()); the three token lists are concatenated tools->system->messages and cut into block_size-token blocks (argparse default 64; class docstring still says 256), full blocks only, remainder discarded. So block identity is the GPT-4 tokenisation of the Anthropic WIRE JSON, not of a rendered chat template and not of the served model's tokenizer.
   - *unaffected*: Fresh claim: exact tokenisation read from code (wire JSON, GPT-4 BPE per section, 64-token full blocks).
   - anchor `build_minimal_traces.py:L122 @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14)` @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14): "text = json.dumps(normalized, separators=(',', ':'))"

2. Block hash: sha256(f'{salt}:{prev_hash}:{seq_num}:' + ' '.join(token ids)) truncated to 16 hex chars, chained on the previous block's hash starting from prev='0'; each distinct hash gets the next integer id in first-seen order. Position is part of the hash, so equal ids imply equal full prefix, not merely equal block content.
   - *unaffected*: Hash construction read from code; matches TRACE_FORMAT description.
   - anchor `build_minimal_traces.py:L96 @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14)` @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14): "content = f"{self.salt}:{prev_hash}:{seq_num}:" + " ".join(map(str, token_ids))"
   - anchor `build_minimal_traces.py:L97 @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14)` @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14): "return hashlib.sha256(content.encode()).hexdigest()[:16]"
3. A GLOBAL (cross-conversation) id mode exists in code and is the DEFAULT: main() builds one shared_hash_state {hash_to_id, next_id, salt} for the whole batch (commit 8b291c9, 2026-03-27 'Implement global hash_ids'), --local-hash-ids opts into per-conversation maps, and the emitted hash_id_scope field records which. The docs are stale/self-contradicting at this commit: README 'Future Work' still lists 'Global hash_ids' and docs/TRACE_FORMAT.md says IDs are 'Currently ... per-conversation', while the README trace-format section and both shipped sample traces say hash_id_scope 'global'. The Stage-1 premise 'global scope is future work and no published trace uses it' is therefore wrong for code and for at least the SemiAnalysis swebench-cc-traces-anon set (see entry for that dataset).
   - *overturned*: intake-2417#record claim 2 said global scope is documented future work with no code/trace; code default IS global since 2026-03-27.
   - anchor `build_minimal_traces.py:L1043 @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14)` @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14): "# Global (default): shared across all conversations — same content = same ID."
   - anchor `build_minimal_traces.py:L1013 @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14)` @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14): "help='Use per-conversation hash_ids instead of global (each conversation gets its own ID namespace)')"
   - anchor `docs/TRACE_FORMAT.md:L113 @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14)` @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14): "Currently, IDs are assigned **per-conversation** — the same content in different conversations gets different numeric IDs."
   - anchor `README.md:L258 @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14)` @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14): "- **Global hash_ids** — Share hash-to-ID mapping across conversations so traces have common IDs for shared prefixes, enabling cross-conversation cache simulation"
4. Salt is secrets.token_hex(16) generated once per build run and never written out; in global mode the same salt and one id map span every conversation AND every sub-agent builder in the run (process_subagent receives the same shared_hash_state), so ids are consistent across all traces of one batch but not across batches. Parent and sub-agent therefore share one namespace in the generator, contradicting TRACE_FORMAT's 'sub-agents run in isolated cache contexts' (that sentence describes the replay model, not the id space).
   - *narrowed*: Salt/namespace scope: batch-wide, includes sub-agents in generator; docs' isolated-cache sentence is about replay semantics.
   - anchor `build_minimal_traces.py:L1050 @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14)` @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14): "shared_hash_state = {'hash_to_id': {}, 'next_id': 1, 'salt': global_salt}"
   - anchor `build_minimal_traces.py:L791 @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14)` @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14): "builder = MinimalTraceBuilder(args.block_size, shared_hash_state=shared_hash_state, salt=salt)"
   - anchor `docs/TRACE_FORMAT.md:L202 @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14)` @ callanjfox/agentic-coding-analysis@15e88f72f902c7852c330caf2cc11722e5dbc1aa (master HEAD, 2026-04-14): "Sub-agents run in **isolated cache contexts**:"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): read build_minimal_traces.py (full relevant paths), kv_common.py, README, TRACE_FORMAT at 15e88f72f902. OVERTURNS the carried-over premise that a global (cross-session) id mode does not exist in code: it exists, is the default since commit 8b291c9 (2026-03-27), and the stale docs are what say otherwise. It does not make the published WEKA set cross-session (those carry hash_id_scope local per intake-2417#record/2418), but the anon swebench set uses global (see its entry).

The -raw set DOES carry full prompt content: each request object holds the complete Anthropic request, i.e. 'system' text blocks (the Claude Code system prompt), 'tools' (full JSON schemas, ~99 KB for ~22K tokens) and 'messages' (user text incl. a SessionStart plugin-hook dump, then growing assistant/tool_result turns), plus t/type/model/in/out/stop/api_time/think_time; header fields block_size -1 and hash_id_scope '' (no hash_ids). Sessions are automated Claude Agent SDK runs (cc_entrypoint=sdk-cli, cc_version 2.1.87, claude-sonnet-4-6), not interactive developer sessions. This is the only public Claude Code trace set we found that supports real prefix-content analysis, but only ~10 sessions.

2. Within one raw session (6 requests in the 1 MB head, 5 parsed complete) tools and every system block except the first are byte-identical across requests and 'messages' is append-only (each request's message list starts with all earlier ones), but the FIRST system block is 'x-anthropic-billing-header: cc_version=...; cc_entrypoint=sdk-cli; cch=<5 hex>;' and its cch value differs on EVERY request (6 distinct values in 6 requests). Any consumer that hashes or sends the system array verbatim therefore sees a prefix that diverges at the start of the system block (~token 22,005 here) on every request.

The -anon files are hash-only and use hash_id_scope 'global' with one shared counter (e92e41ef-3a9: _analysis.unique_blocks 482,629 while the trace has 19,616 block references and 11,704 distinct ids; ids 1-344 are the tools-prefix blocks, later blocks are ~471K+). This contradicts the carried-over 'no published trace uses global scope'. In that trace, all 23 consecutive request pairs share exactly the first 344 blocks (the tools prefix plus the first 11 system tokens) and then diverge, so its trace-derived prefix-match hit is 7,912/19,616 = 40.3% (README/cards of the older sets report ~96%), consistent with the per-request cch value (not token-level reproduced).

### Claims (as verified)

0. Rollout files are JSONL under ~/.codex/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-<UTC-second-ts>-<thread-uuid>[_<rollout-uuid>].jsonl (archived_sessions/ sibling). Each line is {timestamp (ms, Z), optional ordinal, type, payload} where type is a snake_case tag from a closed set: session_meta, response_item (payload plus optional harness metadata), inter_agent_communication, inter_agent_communication_metadata, compacted, turn_context, token_usage_record, world_state, retained_context, security_risk_score, event_msg (payload is itself type-tagged, e.g. token_count, task_started, task_complete, item_completed; the turn_started/turn_complete names are serde ALIASES accepted on read, the written tags are task_started/task_complete), realtime_item. [rollout_payload.rs:L31-L72; recorder.rs:L2113-L2120; recorder.rs:L1760; rollout_file_name.rs]
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from this read

1. Token semantics: Codex input_tokens INCLUDES cached_input_tokens (non_cached_input() = input - cached), and the Responses-API fixture shows cache_write_input_tokens is also inside input_tokens (input 100 = cached 40 + write 60); reasoning_output_tokens is a subset of output_tokens and total_tokens = input + output (110 = 100 + 10). So Codex is the INCLUSIVE convention, opposite to Claude Code / Anthropic and opencode (which store input non-cached). [protocol.rs:L2575-L2581; codex-api/src/sse/responses.rs:L868-L891]
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from this read

2. Two usage records exist and must not be mixed: (a) event_msg/token_count carries info{total_token_usage (cumulative across the thread, built by add_assign of each response), last_token_usage (the latest response), model_context_window} plus rate_limits, with info optional (rate-limit-only updates) - summing token_count lines over-counts; (b) top-level token_usage_record, one per completed Responses API response, carries thread_id, session_id, turn_id, root_turn_id, response_id, usage (this response), turn_token_usage and thread_token_usage (running totals). The per-response record is the clean unit for a projector; response_id is the join key to gen_ai.response.id. [protocol.rs:L2413-L2423; protocol.rs:L2460-L2463; core/src/state/session.rs:L229-L267]
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from this read
   - anchor `codex-rs/protocol/src/protocol.rs:L2413-L2423 @ b337fe06` @ openai/codex@b337fe06b7d8d6e5b42295e04c5e92c2b9c46567: "pub struct TokenUsageRecord { pub thread_id: ThreadId, pub turn_id: String, pub session_id: SessionId, pub root_turn_id: String, pub response_id: String, pub usage: TokenUsage, pub turn_token_usage: TokenUsage, pub thread_token_usage: TokenUsage, }"
   - anchor `codex-rs/protocol/src/protocol.rs:L2460-L2463 @ b337fe06` @ openai/codex@b337fe06b7d8d6e5b42295e04c5e92c2b9c46567: "pub fn append_last_usage(&mut self, last: &TokenUsage) { self.total_token_usage.add_assign(last); self.last_token_usage = last.clone(); }"
3. Stable ids and parent/child linkage: session_meta.session_id equals the ROOT thread's id (older rollouts lacking it get id copied in by the deserializer); id is the thread id (UUIDv7); a sub-agent thread carries parent_thread_id, forked_from_id (+ordinal), session_meta.source = {subagent:{thread_spawn:{parent_thread_id, depth, agent_path, agent_nickname, agent_role}}} and subagent_history_start_ordinal (earlier lines are inherited parent context, not child work - a projector that counts them double-counts parent turns/usage). the task_started event (wire tag; alias turn_started) carries turn_id, root_turn_id and an optional trace_id (the OTel trace id - the join key from rollout to codex OTel spans; ABSENT in the live 0.163.0 rollout sampled here, where OTel is off, so it cannot be relied on). Tool calls are keyed by call_id on function_call / function_call_output (also custom_tool_call, local_shell_call); the optional response-item id is prefix_uuidv7. SQLite thread_spawn_edges(parent_thread_id, child_thread_id PRIMARY KEY, status) mirrors the tree. [protocol.rs:L3283-L3293; L2323-L2337; models.rs FunctionCall; migrations/0021]

4. Schema versioning: there is NO schema/format version field anywhere in rollout, history, protocol or state source (grep for schema_version|format_version|rollout_version|rollout_format returns only a sqlite PRAGMA test). Evolution is by serde default/alias/skip_serializing_if (old rollouts remain readable), session_meta.cli_version (writer version), and ThreadHistoryMode {legacy, paginated} in session_meta.history_mode: paginated rollouts stamp a per-line ordinal and persist item_completed TurnItems, legacy rollouts persist the older user_message/agent_message/exec events instead and no ordinal. SQLite files are versioned by FILENAME (state_5.sqlite with 61 numbered migrations; thread_history_1.sqlite with 8). A projector must key on (cli_version, history_mode, presence of ordinal), not on a version field. [sqlite.rs:L47-L48; protocol.rs:L885-L889; ordinal.rs:L37]

5. The SQLite stores are derived indexes; the JSONL is authoritative. state_5.sqlite threads(id, rollout_path, ... tokens_used, history_mode, thread_source ...) points at the rollout file; thread_history_1.sqlite (thread_turns, thread_items(item_json), thread_history_projection_state.next_rollout_byte_offset/next_rollout_ordinal) is an incrementally replayed projection of paginated rollouts. Persistence is lossy by policy: command output and MCP results are truncated to 64 KiB, and ExecCommandEnd/ExecCommandBegin/Error/streaming deltas are transient (not written), so tool-result fidelity in the rollout is bounded. A trace projector should read JSONL and use SQLite only for the spawn-edge tree and fast thread lookup. [state/thread_history_migrations/0001:L34-L37; policy.rs:L17; policy.rs persisted_event_msg]
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from this read

   - anchor `codex-rs/rollout/src/policy.rs:L17-L17 @ b337fe06` @ openai/codex@b337fe06b7d8d6e5b42295e04c5e92c2b9c46567: "pub(super) const PERSISTED_COMMAND_OUTPUT_MAX_BYTES: usize = 64 * 1024;"

## intake-2439#record — ggml-cpu: FA split across kv for faster TG

- **URL**: https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/19209

- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2425#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. CPU FA decode is split across the KV/context dimension: each thread owns a KV chunk, keeps partial (max, sum, V-accumulator) for ALL query heads, and a final reduction merges the partials (flash-decoding) (PR body).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim, read from source
   - anchor `PR #19209 body (pr19209.txt:L7)` @ PR #19209 merged 2026-02-02 as 9f682fb64 (ancestor of v10 ffc1bac82); API JSON retrieved 2026-10-22, pr19209.txt sha256-12 4d0a74ab137f: "This PR introduces chunking across the context size, where each thread maintains partial accumulators for the sum, maximum, and running VKQ sum for the the soft-max calculation and calculates this for all query heads."
1. Before this PR the CPU FA kernel parallelised only over query rows (= query heads at N=1), so going from 32 to 64 threads gave nothing when n_heads < n_threads (PR body).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim, read from source
   - anchor `PR #19209 body (pr19209.txt:L9)` @ PR #19209 merged 2026-02-02 as 9f682fb64 (ancestor of v10 ffc1bac82); API JSON retrieved 2026-10-22, pr19209.txt sha256-12 4d0a74ab137f: "Also note that going from 32->64 cores on master doesn't make a difference in some cases because we only parallelize the query heads which can be < n_threads"
2. Reported decode gain grows with context, >2x at depth; figure (read from the PNG, approx +-0.3 t/s): llama-8B Q4_K_M 64t ~3.0 -> ~6.7 t/s at 32K, ~5.3 -> ~10.2 at 16K; deepseek2-30B-A3B 64t ~2.6 -> ~7.7 at 32K; gpt-oss-20B 64t ~9.1 -> ~13.6 at 32K. KV type of the bench is NOT stated in the source (llama-bench default f16 presumed, not verified).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim, read from source
   - anchor `PR #19209 body (pr19209.txt:L9) + figure benchmark_comparison_log (pr19209_fig.png sha256-12 f2ffef3105c1)` @ PR #19209 merged 2026-02-02 as 9f682fb64 (ancestor of v10 ffc1bac82); API JSON retrieved 2026-10-22, pr19209.txt sha256-12 4d0a74ab137f: "Tested on three models and the results are good, larger (>2x) speed-ups as context size grows."
3. The optimised path engages only for f16/f32 KV (author, 2026-02-03); q8_0/bf16 KV keep the old vec kernel.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim, read from source
   - anchor `PR #19209 comment am17an 2026-02-03T02:46:01Z (pr19209.txt:L160)` @ PR #19209 merged 2026-02-02 as 9f682fb64 (ancestor of v10 ffc1bac82); API JSON retrieved 2026-10-22, pr19209.txt sha256-12 4d0a74ab137f: "at this point optimised FA path is only used for kv f16 or f32."
4. The PR added a CPU 'use_ref' reference flag so test-backend-ops -b CPU compares the optimised CPU FA against the simple vec kernel (CPU-vs-ref pattern).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim, read from source
   - anchor `PR #19209 comment ggerganov 2026-02-02T09:33:41Z (pr19209.txt:L53)` @ PR #19209 merged 2026-02-02 as 9f682fb64 (ancestor of v10 ffc1bac82); API JSON retrieved 2026-10-22, pr19209.txt sha256-12 4d0a74ab137f: "It runs two CPU backends against each other. We just have to figure out a way to make one of the runs to use the vec kernel."
5. In v10 (ffc1bac82) the path engages iff GGML_FA_SPLIT_KV is not 0 (local INF-70 patch c51e4dabf, default ON), !use_ref, N==1, single sequence (neq3==1), K/V f16 or f32 with k.type==v.type, Q f32, padded n_kv >= 512; chunk = ceil(n_kv/nth).

## intake-2442#record — CPU: faster FA

- **URL**: https://github.com/ikawrakow/ik_llama.cpp/pull/797
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty low, relevance low; categories: kv_cache, hardware_optimization, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-2425#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Uses the KQ mask to skip fully masked parts of ik's CPU FA kernels; up to ~10% PP gain on tested models (Ryzen 7950X, Qwen3-30B-A3B IQ2_XXS, q8_0 K).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim, read from source
   - anchor `ik PR #797 body (ik797.txt:L10)` @ ik_llama.cpp PR #797 merged 2025-09-26 as c108e4b7c; ik797.txt 63d94c5f445f, ik797.diff 95fd12362ecd, retrieved 2026-10-22: "This results in noticeable PP performance gains of up to ~10% for the tested models."
1. Mechanism (diff): reads the mask row of the LAST query in the q_step tile, walks back from the end in k_step blocks while the block's FIRST mask entry is non-zero, and truncates the KV loop there - a trailing-suffix (causal) truncation; one q8_0 path instead breaks at the first block whose first entry is masked. Interior masked blocks are not skipped.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim, read from source
   - anchor `ggml/src/iqk/fa/iqk_fa_templates.h hunk @1355 (ik797.diff:L12)` @ ik_llama.cpp PR #797 merged 2025-09-26 as c108e4b7c; ik797.txt 63d94c5f445f, ik797.diff 95fd12362ecd, retrieved 2026-10-22: "for (; ik >=0 && Mc[ik] != 0; ik -= k_step);"
2. TG is unchanged by the PR (N_KV 7168: 25.09 -> 25.03 t/s).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim, read from source
   - anchor `ik PR #797 body, PR table (ik797.txt:L38)` @ ik_llama.cpp PR #797 merged 2025-09-26 as c108e4b7c; ik797.txt 63d94c5f445f, ik797.diff 95fd12362ecd, retrieved 2026-10-22: "| 1024 | 256 | 7168 | 4.065 | 251.91 | 10.228 | 25.03 |"
3. ik (after #794/#796/#797) vs mainline e789095 (Sept 2025) at N_KV 7168, q8_0 K: PP 253.17 vs 34.28 t/s, TG 25.97 vs 13.08 t/s - a pre-#19012/#19209 mainline baseline.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim, read from source
   - anchor `ik PR #797 comment ikawrakow 2025-09-24T15:33:43Z (ik797.txt:L79)` @ ik_llama.cpp PR #797 merged 2025-09-26 as c108e4b7c; ik797.txt 63d94c5f445f, ik797.diff 95fd12362ecd, retrieved 2026-10-22: "| 1024 | 256 | 7168 | 29.873 | 34.28 | 19.574 | 13.08 |"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): diff read; the skip is a trailing-suffix truncation keyed on the first column of each k_step block of the last query row, so it does not help (and as written would be UNSAFE for) top-k sparse masks whose block-first cell can be -inf while later cells are live. Mainline's vec kernel already skips -inf per cell.

## intake-2443#record — TransluceAI/docent: Docent, an AI agent analysis platform (rubric search, clustering and transcript viewer for agent runs)

0. The LLM layer is provider-registry based (anthropic, google, openai, azure_openai in providers/registry.py; the docs list only the first three). The openai provider builds AsyncOpenAI() with no base_url argument, so pointing it at a local OpenAI-compatible server relies on the openai SDK reading OPENAI_BASE_URL (confirmed in the installed openai 2.31.0 _client.py:184), one base URL per process for every model whose provider is "openai". No local/vLLM/ollama/base_url option exists anywhere in the repo (grep over *.py,*.md,*.yml,*.toml).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `docent_core/_llm_util/providers/openai.py:L427 @ 07edc43` @ TransluceAI/docent @ 07edc43f256365c1e6d0c779a624f60fb1893f9a (2026-10-19), shallow clone read 2026-10-22: "return AsyncOpenAI(api_key=api_key) if api_key else AsyncOpenAI()"
1. Model choice is hard-coded per function in providers/preferences.py (default_judge_models starts gpt-5 medium, then gpt-5 low, gpt-5-mini, claude-sonnet-4; clustering uses o3-mini/o4-mini/sonnet/gemini-flash); the documented customisation route is editing that file, so a local judge needs a source patch (a ModelOption with provider "openai" and the local alias) rather than a config switch. Unknown model names only trigger a 100k default-context warning (MODEL_CONTEXT_WINDOWS).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `docs/self_hosting/llm_providers_and_calls.md:L55 @ 07edc43` @ TransluceAI/docent @ 07edc43f256365c1e6d0c779a624f60fb1893f9a (2026-10-19), shallow clone read 2026-10-22: "3. Specify the [`ModelOption`][docent_core._llm_util.providers.preferences.ModelOption] objects in the returned list"
2. The rubric judge (ai_tools/rubric/rubric.py) is prompt-only: the whole run is rendered by AgentRun.to_text_new() into one user message ("reason through the rubric, then output JSON matching this schema"), the reply is json.loads-ed and jsonschema-validated, unparseable replies become None. No response_format/grammar is passed and neither rubric.py, AgentRun.to_text_new() nor prod_llms.py applies a token cap (grep for truncate: no hits in prod_llms.py), so a small local model loses rows on malformed JSON and long Claude Code sessions must be segmented before judging. llama-server (v10 tree ffc1bac8, tools/server/server-schema.cpp:46) aliases max_completion_tokens to n_predict, so the call itself is accepted.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `docent_core/docent/ai_tools/rubric/rubric.py:L286 @ 07edc43` @ TransluceAI/docent @ 07edc43f256365c1e6d0c779a624f60fb1893f9a (2026-10-19), shallow clone read 2026-10-22: "parsed_output = json.loads(output.first_text) if output.first_text else None"
3. Transcript schema: AgentRun{id,name,description,transcripts:list[Transcript],transcript_groups,metadata} with Transcript{messages:list[ChatMessage],metadata,created_at}; ChatMessage is a role-discriminated union (system/user/assistant/tool) with assistant tool_calls (id, function, arguments:dict), ContentText/ContentReasoning blocks and free-form metadata dicts. The only file loader shipped is Inspect .eval (loaders/load_inspect.py) plus an OpenTelemetry tracing SDK; zero hits for claude-code/codex/opencode/jsonl in *.py/*.md, so Claude Code, Codex and opencode transcripts need an adapter that we write, and the schema can represent them (tool_use becomes tool_calls, tool_result becomes tool messages, interrupt markers stay as user text).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `docent/docent/data_models/chat/message.py:L25 @ 07edc43` @ TransluceAI/docent @ 07edc43f256365c1e6d0c779a624f60fb1893f9a (2026-10-19), shallow clone read 2026-10-22: "role: Literal["system", "user", "assistant", "tool"]"
4. Hosted/other dependencies are avoidable for the judge path: self-hosting is docker-compose with local Postgres and Redis; Sentry and PostHog are off unless DEPLOYMENT_ID is set; import of docent_core raises FileNotFoundError without a .env file. Traps: Docent client and tracing SDK default to https://api.docent.transluce.org (any adapter must override server_url/endpoint or transcripts leave the host), embeddings default to OpenAI text-embedding-3-large, and tiktoken fetches its cl100k BPE from openaipublic.blob.core.windows.net on first use unless TIKTOKEN_CACHE_DIR is pre-seeded. Licence: LICENSE is the 13-line Apache-2.0 notice (Copyright 2025 Clarity AI Research Inc., dba Transluce) and both pyproject files declare Apache-2.0; GitHub API reports NOASSERTION because the full licence text is absent.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `docent/docent/sdk/client.py:L31 @ 07edc43` @ TransluceAI/docent @ 07edc43f256365c1e6d0c779a624f60fb1893f9a (2026-10-19), shallow clone read 2026-10-22: "server_url: str = "https://api.docent.transluce.org","
5. Docent clustering is LLM-driven, not embedding-driven: propose_clusters sends up to 100k tokens of result strings into one prompt and LlmApiClusterAssigner then judges every item against every centroid (items x centroids calls in multi_round_clustering.py), using o3-mini/o4-mini/sonnet/gemini-flash. That is far more calls and context than a local CPU model can serve; the embedding-first design in Clio (intake entry for arXiv 2412.13678) is the local-friendly alternative.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `docent_core/docent/ai_tools/clustering/cluster_generator.py:L82 @ 07edc43` @ TransluceAI/docent @ 07edc43f256365c1e6d0c779a624f60fb1893f9a (2026-10-19), shallow clone read 2026-10-22: "# Truncate items to 100k tokens"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): the Stage-1 note for intake-2428#record said the Docent repo was NOT read. Read at 07edc43: the judge/cluster stack can run against a local OpenAI-compatible endpoint only through OPENAI_BASE_URL plus a preferences.py patch (no first-class base_url or local provider), has no hosted-DB dependency (local Postgres+Redis), ships no Claude Code/Codex/opencode loader, and defaults its SDK to the hosted Docent API.

## intake-2444#record — Clio: Privacy-Preserving Insights into Real-World AI Use

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.13678
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: knowledge_management, benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2429#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Pipeline: (1) per-conversation facet extraction (some facets computed mechanically, some by a model, e.g. a 1-2 sentence request summary at temperature 0.2), (2) embed the summary facet with all-mpnet-base-v2 (768-d) and run k-means, (3) a model names each base cluster from 50 in-cluster plus 50 nearest-outside summaries (contrastive naming), (4) hierarchy by embedding cluster names, k-means into neighbourhoods of ~40, then LLM propose/deduplicate/assign/rename per level (the authors found HDBSCAN and agglomerative inferior to this), (5) UMAP map and tree UI. App. B.1-B.4, G.4-G.7.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Sec. 2.2 item 2 (Semantic clustering) arXiv:2412.13678v1 (submitted 2024-12-18), HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 0251dc2918e4` @ arXiv:2412.13678v1 (submitted 2024-12-18), HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 0251dc2918e4: "Clio then groups similar conversations by creating embeddings"
1. Cost is dominated by facet extraction: for 100,000 conversations the estimate is $48.81, of which Haiku-3 facet extraction is $45.00 (130.0M input tokens, 10.0M output; about 92% by my arithmetic), cluster labelling $3.75 and hierarchy $0.06 (Table 3, assuming 1,000-token conversations and 100-conversation clusters). The extraction step is therefore the one a small local model must carry; the paper reports no open or small-local-model result (NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Sec. 2.2 "Cost of a Clio run"; Table 3 arXiv:2412.13678v1 (submitted 2024-12-18), HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 0251dc2918e4` @ arXiv:2412.13678v1 (submitted 2024-12-18), HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 0251dc2918e4: "For our example run of 100,000 conversations, the cost of such a run is $48.81"
2. Reported validation, all with Claude 3/3.5 and Anthropic reviewers: 200 conversations manually checked, 96% of summaries accurate (93% random, 98% flagged); 5,904 cluster assignments in 183 clusters, 99% (random) and 96% (flagged) of titles accurate with about 3% of members not clearly belonging; hierarchy titles 97%; synthetic reconstruction of 19,476 conversations over 20 categories 94% (84% on the 18-category concerning set). Dominant failure modes: long multi-topic conversations drop topics, and cluster titles on harmful content are too generic.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `App. C.1.1 Conversation summaries` @ arXiv:2412.13678v1 (submitted 2024-12-18), HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 0251dc2918e4: "We found that 96% of the conversations were summarized accurately"
3. Privacy design has four layers (summary prompt omits private data, minimum-size thresholds on unique accounts and conversations, cluster-summary prompt, LLM cluster audit), is empirical not formal (no differential privacy or k-anonymity guarantee), and the numeric thresholds and k are withheld by the authors (NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE). The safety-monitoring use deliberately drops the thresholds and audit so enforcement can reach individual accounts; for a local pass over our own transcripts none of the privacy layers is needed, but the unique-account threshold has a methodological analogue (minimum distinct sessions per cluster).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `App. G.5 Base-Level Clusterer` @ arXiv:2412.13678v1 (submitted 2024-12-18), HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 0251dc2918e4: "we unfortunately cannot provide our precise values for"
4. Self-stated limits matter for failure-mode mining: outputs are leads needing validation before decisions, absence of a pattern cannot be proved, and clustering "is not useful for identifying rare (but possibly very consequential) patterns", so rare high-severity failures (a single destructive command) need mechanical detectors, not clusters (Sec. 5.1-5.2).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Sec. 5.2 item 5` @ arXiv:2412.13678v1 (submitted 2024-12-18), HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 0251dc2918e4: "Clio will only identify patterns across many different conversations, which means it is not useful for identifying rare (but possibly very consequential) patterns"
5. Unit of analysis is the chat conversation (cost model assumes 1,000 tokens per conversation; Clio "only analyzes patterns within conversations"). Agentic coding sessions are far longer and contain tool traces, so a transcript pass must cluster segments (task windows or tool-error episodes), not whole sessions; Anthropic's own classifiers over Claude Code sessions truncate each turn to 5,000 characters and each session to 25,000 characters (expertise-report appendix).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Table 3 notes` @ arXiv:2412.13678v1 (submitted 2024-12-18), HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 0251dc2918e4: "Assumptions: average conversation length of 1,000 tokens"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): intake-2429#record listed the Clio paper as NOT read. Read the arXiv HTML v1 (abstract, Sec. 1-6 headings and text, App. B, C, G.4-G.7 and Table 3). The privacy thresholds are described but never quantified, and the paper contains no small-model or open-model result, so how well a small local model can do facet extraction is unmeasured by this source.

- created `handoffs/active/dev-harness-trace-mining.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/unified-trace-memory-service.md`

## intake-2445#record — Agentic coding and persistent returns to expertise

- **URL**: https://www.anthropic.com/research/claude-code-expertise
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-2429#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Premise correction: the report does not study tenure or interrupts at all (zero occurrences of "interrupt" or "tenure" in the page text, the appendix text and the main-report PDF text). Its covariate is a per-session, task-specific expertise rating (1 novice to 5 expert, by a Sonnet 4.6 classifier from three co-equal transcript signals: setup specificity, targeted-versus-generic verification asks, and direction of correction). Account age (days since Anthropic signup) is listed only as a recorded telemetry field. The tenure and interrupt effect (5% to 9% per turn) lives in intake-2429#record, not here.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Section "Level of expertise"` @ Anthropic Economics page published 2026-06-16, HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 c0506ed6e386: "From each transcript, Claude rates the user's apparent expertise at the task on a five-point scale from novice to expert."
1. Expertise changes how much the agent does per instruction: a typical novice prompt sets off about 5 actions and about 600 words, an expert prompt 12 actions and 3,200 words; the gradient stays at +9% actions and +13% output per expertise level in a regression controlling for work mode, task value, month, occupation and model family with standard errors clustered by user. Consequence for any rate: events per turn and events per tool call move differently as the operator's specificity changes.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Section "Level of expertise" (Figure 3 text)` @ Anthropic Economics page published 2026-06-16, HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 c0506ed6e386: "In typical novice sessions, each prompt sets off about five Claude actions and roughly 600 words of output, while expert sessions set off action chains more than twice as long (12 actions) carrying five times the output (3,200 words) (Figure 3)."
2. Success gradient (observational, matched on work mode, task value, month, subject and occupation group): novice-rated sessions reach verified success 15% of the time (at least partial 77%) versus 28-33% (91-92%) for intermediate and above; among sessions that hit trouble verified success rises from 4% (novice) to 15% (expert); 19% of troubled novice sessions are abandoned with zero code written versus 5-7% for others. Footnote 9: conditioning on trouble selects harder problems for experts, so part of the recovery gap may be selection. On internal Anthropic sessions joined to landed commits the gradient replicates within engineer (author fixed effects), roughly 3 to 5 points of judged success per level (App. p.22).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Section "The returns to expertise" (Figure 5 text)` @ Anthropic Economics page published 2026-06-16, HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 c0506ed6e386: "A novice-rated session reaches our strictest measure, verified success, 15% of the time and at least partial success 77% of the time."
   - anchor `Appendix p. 22 (Internal outcome validation)` @ Appendix PDF cdn.sanity.io a94728142a45_694_292_336_165_947f8d6e3e1a357e.pdf, sha256-12 43139a8128ed, text via stdlib literal-string extractor (ligature and dash glyphs preserved as Unicode): "roughly 35 percentage points per expertise level with author"
3. Classifier validity is weaker than the headline suggests: against a stronger reference model (Mythos Preview) on 198 SWE-chat sessions the report's classifiers agree on 78-98% of categorical labels, but on ordinal scales (expertise is one) only 53-68% counting exact matches and 78-99% counting adjacent points; the authors say conclusions rest on relative comparisons, and a human audited 15 flagged disagreements and concurred in 11. "Verified success" requires a judged success plus at least one hard signal (commits or PRs, passing tests, explicit user affirmation).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Appendix p. 23 (Classifier validation via strong-model agreement)` @ Appendix PDF cdn.sanity.io a94728142a45_694_292_336_165_947f8d6e3e1a357e.pdf, sha256-12 43139a8128ed, text via stdlib literal-string extractor (ligature and dash glyphs preserved as Unicode): "reference agree on 78-99% counting adjacent scale points as agreement, and 53-68% counting only exact matches as agreement."
4. The workload mix drifts inside the observation window: the share of sessions spent fixing broken code fell from 33% to 19% between October 2025 and April 2026 while operating software rose from 14% to 21% and average estimated session value rose 27%, i.e. composition, models and products changed under the series (a history threat for any pre/post design; the authors absorb it with month and model-family controls).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Section "The work" (Figure 4 text)` @ Anthropic Economics page published 2026-06-16, HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 c0506ed6e386: "The clearest change is that the share of sessions spent fixing broken code fell from 33% to 19% (Figure 4)."
5. Scope and exclusions: interactive sessions only (398,198 sessions, 234,751 users, Oct 2025 to Apr 2026, internal Anthropic usage excluded); headless claude -p sessions, IDE integrations and SDK use are excluded, so subagent and automated-pipeline behaviour, which dominate part of our dev-harness traces, is outside the evidence. All numbers come from Anthropic-internal classifiers with no raw data released.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim from the combined pass
   - anchor `Footnote 04` @ Anthropic Economics page published 2026-06-16, HTML retrieved 2026-10-22, sha256-12 c0506ed6e386: "We also therefore exclude sessions in “headless” mode where a user runs a single prompt in the CLI via claude -p"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): the task brief framed this page as tenure/interrupt evidence; it contains neither (grep: 0 hits for interrupt and tenure). Only rated expertise, actions per prompt, success and abandonment are measured. Appendix PDF (a9472814...) read; the "Read in PDF" copy of the main report was extracted (29.8k chars, 0 hits for interrupt/tenure) and then deleted as a duplicate of the HTML page; the internal within-engineer replication on appendix p.22 reads 3 to 5 percentage points of judged success per expertise level with author fixed effects (an en dash that a terminal display collapses to 35; checked byte-level). | 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (operator pre-approved all recommended sources): dive-surfaced sources NOT dived, with reasons: arXiv 2601.18341 and 2606.07448 (agent-adoption base rates) held for operator confirmation before decline (plan Q7); not dived.

### Claims (as verified)

0. MIT-licensed Clio reimplementation whose LLM interface is hard-bound to an in-process vllm.LLM (runClio signature, tokenizer.apply_chat_template prompt building, llm.generate with vllm.SamplingParams); there is no OpenAI-compatible/base_url path, so it cannot target a llama-server endpoint without rewriting its 4 LLM call sites and prompt builder.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `README.md L4 @ 28ec1d8` @ 28ec1d8fc07d196d15cf142c6dbc3c82fad1643d (main, retrieved 2026-10-22): "Designed to run using local LLMs via [VLLM](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm)."
1. Pipeline: dedup -> per-conversation facet extraction (LLM) -> facet-value embedding (SentenceTransformer, README example all-mpnet-base-v2) -> FAISS k-means base clusters (n//10) -> LLM names/summaries per cluster (5 resamples, medoid pick) -> neighborhood k-means -> LLM higher-level names -> dedup -> LLM assignment -> rename, until <=5 top clusters; i.e. embed-first like Clio, with LLM facet extraction BEFORE embedding (embeds facet values, not raw conversations).
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `openclio/openclio.py L85-L87, L367 @ 28ec1d8` @ 28ec1d8fc07d196d15cf142c6dbc3c82fad1643d (main, retrieved 2026-10-22): "def runClio(facets: List[Facet], llm: vllm.LLM, embeddingModel: SentenceTransformer,"
2. Inputs are lists of {role,content} turns (conversationToString, truncated at 8000 tokens on assistant-turn boundaries); non-conversation data is supported via a custom Facet.getFacetPrompt or clio.genericSummaryFacets with a dataToStr callable, so transcript segments can be fed by mapping to role/content turns.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `openclio/prompts.py L62-L76 @ 28ec1d8` @ 28ec1d8fc07d196d15cf142c6dbc3c82fad1643d (main, retrieved 2026-10-22): "def conversationToString(conversation: List[Dict[str, str]], tokenizer, maxTokens: int=-1) -> str:"
3. No quality/accuracy numbers are reported in the README (only a demo run on ~400k WildChat conversations); the repo ships no evaluation of cluster quality.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `README.md full text (absence) @ 28ec1d8` @ 28ec1d8fc07d196d15cf142c6dbc3c82fad1643d (main, retrieved 2026-10-22): "NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE: no quality metrics in README (searched README.md, project-wiki/*.md headings)"
4. GPU dependence is vllm (hard dependency in pyproject along with torch); embedding and FAISS k-means run on CPU-capable libs (faiss-cpu), so only the LLM side is the blocker for our local pass.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `pyproject.toml dependencies @ 28ec1d8` @ 28ec1d8fc07d196d15cf142c6dbc3c82fad1643d (main, retrieved 2026-10-22): ""vllm", "pandas", "sentence_transformers","

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): intake-2444#record notes said OpenClio was only README-skimmed; source read shows it is vLLM-in-process only (not OpenAI-compatible) and embeds facet values after LLM extraction. Not a drop-in for a llama-server endpoint; reusable as a reference for pipeline shape and prompts (prompts.py, 454 lines).

## intake-2450#record — Fused matrix multiplications (CUDA and CPU)

- **URL**: https://github.com/ikawrakow/ik_llama.cpp/pull/796
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty low, relevance low; categories: hardware_optimization, local_inference, inference_serving)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-2442#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. PR body: adds the ability to fuse quantized matrix multiplications that operate on the same activations A (typically the Q, K and V projections) on CUDA and CPU; it does not fuse them into one kernel, it saves the quantization of A and the thread synchronization between the matmuls.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `PR #796 body para 1` @ ikawrakow/ik_llama.cpp PR #796 merge 8e497e704e733e0a14df0eee284b51c92e11ab29; API body retrieved 2026-10-22 sha256-12 065232c36802: "The implementation does not go as far as fusing these into a single kernel, but it does save the quantization of $A$ into the required type, along with the thread synchronization between such matrix multiplications."
1. The author reports the performance gain as surprisingly small, typically <= 1%, and adds it as a micro-optimization.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `PR #796 body para 2` @ ikawrakow/ik_llama.cpp PR #796 merge 8e497e704e733e0a14df0eee284b51c92e11ab29; API body retrieved 2026-10-22 sha256-12 065232c36802: "The performance gain is surprisingly small, typically being $\le 1$%."
2. Mechanism (diff, ggml.c): after one MUL_MAT completes via iqk_mul_mat_4d, a while-loop runs the following graph nodes while they are MUL_MAT with the same src1 and the same vec_dot_type, reusing the already-quantized wdata; ggml_compute_forward_mul_mat returns the advanced node index. src/llama.cpp adds llm_build_mul_mat_qkv so Q/K/V matmuls are adjacent in the graph.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `pr796.diff ggml.c hunk @@ -15163 (fusion loop)` @ ikawrakow/ik_llama.cpp PR #796 merge 8e497e704e733e0a14df0eee284b51c92e11ab29; pulls/796.diff retrieved 2026-10-22 sha256-12 7a1a1380453d: "cgraph->nodes[node_n+1]->src[1] == src1 &&"
3. Scope: no flash-attention code; the premise that #796 belongs to ik's CPU FA work is wrong.
   - *unaffected*: fresh claim
   - anchor `pr796.diff file list (3 files: ggml-cuda.cu, ggml.c, src/llama.cpp; no FA file)` @ ikawrakow/ik_llama.cpp PR #796 merge 8e497e704e733e0a14df0eee284b51c92e11ab29; pulls/796.diff retrieved 2026-10-22 sha256-12 7a1a1380453d: "diff --git a/src/llama.cpp b/src/llama.cpp"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): this PR is NOT flash attention (files: ggml-cuda.cu, ggml.c, llama.cpp). It fuses consecutive quantized MUL_MATs that read the same activations (Q/K/V projections) so the activation is quantized once and one thread barrier per matmul is saved; the author himself reports <=1%. The stage-1 premise that #796 is part of ik's CPU FA work is overturned. Whether the saved barrier is worth more at 96 threads than at the 16-thread 7950X is a hypothesis, not in the source.

## intake-2452#record — Better TG performance for GQA models (CPU)

- **URL**: https://github.com/ikawrakow/ik_llama.cpp/pull/332

0. Mechanism (diff, iqk_flash_attn.cpp): a new TG-only FA branch (neq1==1, neq3==1, rk2>1) makes work units of (KV head x KV-chunk) with chunk nk = 32*floor(nek2*nek1/(32*nth)) positions, strides the units over threads, and per unit calls the FA kernel with rk2 Q rows (all query heads of that KV head) in ONE K/V pass; per-unit (R, M, S) partials go to a work buffer, a barrier follows, and each thread then merges the nkk partials of its heads with the online-softmax rescale. - 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): REATTRIBUTED. The prior dive credited #171 with 'multi-row Q tiles'; the diff is a thread-distribution fix (when to split rows of one head across threads), not a tile change. Its transferable content is a negative rule about row-splitting at small neq1, which matters for v10 because v10 assigns every Q row to its own thread.

## intake-2455#record — cpu: accumulate flash attention V in F32 in the one-chunk kernel

- **URL**: https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/29810
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: already_integrated (novelty low, relevance high; categories: kv_cache, hardware_optimization, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2439#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Issue #29774 (open): ggml-cpu one-chunk FA accumulates the un-normalised V sum in F16 (VKQ16), which overflows to -inf then NaN; repro on Nemotron-3-Nano-4B Q8_0, -fa on vs off (issue body).
   - *unaffected*: Issue confirmed by the PR author on the same model; CUDA clean.
   - anchor `issue #29774 body (Problem description)` @ PR head 6c133191c / issue #29774, retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "I believe this happens because it uses a F16 accumulator instead of a F32 at ggml/src/ggml-cpu/ops.cpp:ggml_compute_forward_flash_attn_ext_f16_one_chunk"
1. Mechanism: the tiled kernel (>= GGML_FA_TILE_Q=64 query rows) accumulates in F32; the one-chunk kernel (decode, verify/spec batches, prompt tails < 64 rows) kept an F16 running sum normalised only at the end, so it overflows past 65504 on long contexts (PR body).
   - *narrowed*: Stage-brief phrasing 'small batches take the F16 kernel' is exact only for F16 V; quantized V (q8_0) in the same kernel accumulates in F32 (v10 L8975-8994).
   - anchor `PR #29810 body, Overview` @ PR head 6c133191c / issue #29774, retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "small ones (decode, or the tail of a prompt) use a one-chunk kernel that accumulated an F16 V cache in an F16 running sum, normalized only at the end. On long contexts that sum overflows past 65504: inf, then NaN on every following token."
2. Trigger in the repro: an 8196-token prompt whose LAST ubatch has 4 rows runs one-chunk over the whole ~8.2k KV of a repetitive prompt (diffuse attention); the first non-finite node is FLASH_ATTN_EXT with D=128 (issue body + log).
   - *unaffected*: Read from the issue repro and log; D=128 in the log.
   - anchor `issue #29774 body, repro script comment` @ PR head 6c133191c / issue #29774, retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "8195 words + BOS = 8196 tokens: the prompt's last batch has 4 tokens, and small batches take the F16 kernel"
3. Fix (PR head 6c133191c, OPEN, unmerged at retrieval; upstream master 7170cad4 2026-10-22 still has VKQ16): every V type accumulates in F32; F16 rows use a new fused convert+FMA ggml_vec_mad_f16_f32 (AVX-512F/F16C, NEON, RVV) (pr29810.diff).
   - *narrowed*: Not merged: PR and issue both OPEN at retrieval; upstream master 7170cad4 still contains VKQ16 (master_ops.cpp L9031).
   - anchor `pr29810.diff ggml/src/ggml-cpu/ops.cpp hunk @@ -8989 @ head 6c133191c` @ PR head 6c133191c / issue #29774, retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "ggml_vec_mad_f16_f32(DV, VKQ32, (const ggml_fp16_t *) v_data, vs);"
4. Cost evidence: a naive F32 fix that converts V rows via the scalar ggml_fp16_to_fp32_row cost -38% single-thread tg64@d4096 on a 9950X3D; the fused version restores 6.85 -> 6.77 t/s (PR comments).
   - *unaffected*: Matches EPYC's independent finding that a two-pass convert costs ~3x FA op time on Zen 5 (kv-unified-stack-rollout.md CPU-FA-VKQ-0).
   - anchor `PR #29810 comment ServeurpersoCom 2026-10-02T18:44:56Z` @ PR head 6c133191c / issue #29774, retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "tg64 @ d4096: 6.85 -> 4.24 t/s (-38%)"
5. Final PR numbers: x86 single-thread tg64 7.55 -> 7.74, tg64@d4096 6.85 -> 6.77; Apple M5 tg64@d4096 10.29 -> 9.86 (-4%, master used native F16 FMA there); FA matches CUDA on 3986/3986 test-backend-ops (PR body). Four alternative fixes were tried (naive F32, F16 folded every 256 positions, CUDA/Vulkan-style FATTN_KQ_MAX_OFFSET, fused F32).
   - *unaffected*: Single-thread, one model (Gemma 4 E4B UD-Q8_K_XL); no multi-thread or D=256 numbers.
   - anchor `PR #29810 body, Overview` @ PR head 6c133191c / issue #29774, retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "x86 (Ryzen 9950X3D): tg64 7.55 -> 7.74 t/s, tg64 @ d4096 6.85 -> 6.77 t/s"
6. EPYC applicability (code read): production v10 ffc1bac82 carries the defect verbatim (ops.cpp L8907-8912, L8959-8973, L9000-9004) on the paths reached from L9464 (split-KV, N=1) and L9543 (neq1 < 64); only F16 V reaches it, q8_0 V takes the F32 VKQ32 branch.
   - *narrowed*: Defect present in v10, but on :8074 the main model's 12 QSA layers see at most 2051 cells; EPYC probe measured acc_peak <= 237.6 to 18,940 tokens (run_base.log). The dense MTP draft head is unbounded.
   - anchor `ggml/src/ggml-cpu/ops.cpp:L8907 @ ffc1bac82 (production v10)` @ PR head 6c133191c / issue #29774, retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "ggml_fp16_t * VKQ16 = (ggml_fp16_t *) (VKQ32 + 1*DV); // (temporary) FP16 VKQ accumulator"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): read issue + PR + diff + all comments; PR is OPEN (not merged), upstream master still affected; v10 ffc1bac82 carries the defect; EPYC already reproduced and fixed it independently (CPU-FA-VKQ-0, champion 9a3f1392a / V11-015), so the new information is the upstream issue/PR to redirect CPU-FA-VKQ-4 and upstream's fused-F32 cost data for CPU-FA-VKQ-2. | 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out after wave 6 (the operator-authorized six-wave limit for this session): surfaced sources NOT dived: ggml-org PR 28576 (MFMA FA F16 overflow fix, GPU analogue) declined for this session by the wave cap and flagged to the operator; PRs 22378 and 22022 declined (THP shim and no-mmap already cover them).

## intake-2456#record — ggml : speed up batch-1 CPU decode, align large allocations

- **URL**: https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/27478
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: hardware_optimization, kv_cache, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-2442#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. One-chunk (vec) FA restructured into KV blocks of GGML_FA_KQ_BLK=32: scores for a block are computed first, the running max/accumulator are rescaled at most once per block, and exp is done by ggml_vec_soft_max_f32 over the block (PR body + ops.cpp diff).
   - *unaffected*: Read from diff (GGML_FA_KQ_BLK 32 in common.h).
   - anchor `PR #27478 body, Overview` @ PR head 0577cbcb4 (base 5b6ddc967), retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "vlocks the KV loop, rescaling once every 32 elements"
1. For F16 K with F32 Q, Q stays F32 (no F16 round trip) and four K rows are dotted per Q load (ggml_vec_dot_f16_f32_x4out); grouping is disabled for quantized K and when masked cells break a group of 4 (diff L 'const int grp').
   - *narrowed*: Stage brief said '4 K.Q per Q load' generally; it only engages for F16 K + F32 Q (Q38FN f16 KV qualifies; q8_0-K roles do not).
   - anchor `pr27478.diff ops.cpp one_chunk hunk @ head 0577cbcb4` @ PR head 0577cbcb4 (base 5b6ddc967), retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "const int grp = (GGML_HAS_INTERDOT_X4 && q_stays_f32 && t + 3 < nb &&"
2. V accumulates in F32 via a fused ggml_vec_mad_f16_f32 whenever AVX-512F or AVX2+F16C+FMA is present, so on x86 the PR also removes the F16 VKQ16 overflow of #29774 as a side effect (diff use_f16_acc).
   - *unaffected*: New finding vs brief: it doubles as an overflow fix on x86.
   - anchor `PR #27478 body, Overview` @ PR head 0577cbcb4 (base 5b6ddc967), retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "Computes four K.Q dot products per Q load and accumulates V in F32 where a mixed multiply-add is used."
3. Qwen3-30B-A3B Q4_K_M tg@8k: Ryzen 7 9700X +15.29% total, attention change alone +10.67%; Neoverse-N1 +9.22% / +7.43%; the rest is a 2 MiB-aligned + MADV_HUGEPAGE allocator change (PR body).
   - *unaffected*: Matches brief's +10.67% attention-only.
   - anchor `PR #27478 body, Additional information` @ PR head 0577cbcb4 (base 5b6ddc967), retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "attention change alone +10.67%"
4. Caveats (PR body): only DV=128 measured end-to-end; DV=256 loses ~3.5% on x86 ONLY in a build with GGML_NO_INTERDOT_X4 (four-output helper off); attention-only change regresses pp512 by 2.194% (attributed to code layout via a dead-code control); 2..63-row fallback not evaluated.
   - *narrowed*: Brief's '-3.5% at D=256 in one build' is the GGML_NO_INTERDOT_X4 opt-out build; D=256 with the default x4 helper is NOT measured (NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE).
   - anchor `PR #27478 body, Scope limits` @ PR head 0577cbcb4 (base 5b6ddc967), retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "DV=256 loses ~3.5% on x86 built with GGML_NO_INTERDOT_X4 (F32-Q dot on, four-output helper off)"
   - anchor `PR #27478 body, Scope limits` @ PR head 0577cbcb4 (base 5b6ddc967), retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "one head geometry measured end-to-end: DV=128"

### Dive corrections

## intake-2457#record — ggml-cpu: enable tiled flash attention for non-vector-multiple head dims on x86

- **URL**: https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/29423
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: not_applicable (novelty low, relevance low; categories: hardware_optimization, multimodal, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-2441#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Before the PR, head dims not a multiple of the SIMD width (e.g. 40, 72) are excluded from the tiled kernel and fall to the per-query one-chunk kernel, which is ~10x slower and accumulates P*V in F16 (PR body).
   - *unaffected*: Ties to #29810: non-multiple dims also hit the F16 accumulator.
   - anchor `PR #29423 body, Overview` @ merge commit 6f767fe96 (head e63e53400), retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "Head sizes like 72 fall back to the per-query kernel, which is an order of magnitude slower and also accumulates P*V in F16."
1. The PR removes the DV % f32_epr gate on x86-64 and handles the GEMM's leftover columns with masked AVX-512 (or AVX2 maskload) loads/stores/FMA instead of scalar per-column loops (simd-gemm.h diff).
   - *unaffected*: Brief summary correct.
   - anchor `PR #29423 body, Overview` @ merge commit 6f767fe96 (head e63e53400), retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "handles the leftover columns of the P*V tile GEMM with masked AVX-512 loads/stores/FMA instead of scalar per-column dot products"
2. FA operator, F16 K/V, 9950X 16 threads: 12-28x faster at D=40/72; Qwen3-VL BF16 mmproj on CPU 2.86-8.28x faster (PR tables).
   - *unaffected*: Author's numbers, x86 only.
   - anchor `PR #29423 body, FA operator table (Ryzen 9 9950X, 16 threads)` @ merge commit 6f767fe96 (head e63e53400), retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "| 72 | 3136 | 399.84 | 24.85 | 16.1x |"
3. Review found and the PR fixed a latent tiled-kernel bug: padded KV entries are set to -inf BEFORE logit soft-capping, which maps them to a finite -softcap so they receive weight when there is no mask (reviewer comment + diff reordering).
   - *unaffected*: v10 carries the same ordering (ops.cpp L9235-9246, padding then softcap); harmless for masked decoder roles.
   - anchor `PR #29423 comment am17an 2026-09-28T10:13:29Z` @ merge commit 6f767fe96 (head e63e53400), retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "Soft-capping gives padded KV entries nonzero attention weight"
4. EPYC applicability: every production LLM head dim (128, 256, 512) is a multiple of 16, so v10's gate (ops.cpp L9532) already admits them to the tiled path; merged 2026-09-28, not in v10.
   - *narrowed*: Brief asked whether it matters for D=256: it does not; the only EPYC-relevant shapes would be CPU vision encoders, which run on MI210 today.
   - anchor `ggml/src/ggml-cpu/ops.cpp:L9532 @ ffc1bac82 (production v10 tiled gate)` @ merge commit 6f767fe96 (head e63e53400), retrieved 2026-10-22T10:33Z: "use_tiled &= (DV % f32_epr == 0);"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-22 Stage-2b dive (combined pass): confirmed no effect on LLM head dims 128/256/512; surfaced the softcap-after-padding latent bug that v10's tiled kernel also carries.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-research-loop.md`
